### XV. THE TRANSLATING AND THE EDITING

Although the translating of this manuscript was done wholly in the presence of the editor in Gangtok, Sikkim <note>Sikkim is a region in the Eastern Himalayas, then a kingdom and now a state of India</note>, the chief credit should be given to the late **spiritual teacher** <note>original: *Lāma*</note> Kazi Dawa-Samdup, the translator. The **spiritual teacher** <note>original: *Lāma*</note> himself aptly summarized the editor’s part in the work by saying that the editor was his living English dictionary. Indeed the editor could have been little more than this, for his knowledge of Tibetan was almost as nothing.

The aim of both the translator and the editor has been to keep as closely to the sense of the text as the idiomatic structures of the Tibetan and English tongues permit. Sometimes the translator, preferring to render into English the real meaning which a **spiritual teacher** <note>original: *lāma*</note> would derive from certain more or less technically-worded phrases, has departed from a strictly literal translation.

The Tibetan of **esoteric ritual** <note>original: *Tantric*</note> texts, such as ours, is especially difficult to turn into good English; and owing to the terseness of many passages it has been necessary to interpolate words and phrases, which are bracketed.

In years to come, it is quite probable that our rendering—as has been the case with the pioneer translations of the *Bible*—may be subject to revision. A strictly literal rendering of a work so abstruse in its real meanings as this, and written in symbolical language as well, if attempted by Europeans—who, finding it difficult to get out of their Western mentality, too often are Christians first and scholars second when working with non-Christian sacred texts—would, perhaps, be as misleading as some of their renderings of the ancient **Knowledge books** <note>original: Sanskrit *Vedas*; the oldest scriptures of Hinduism</note>. Even to a Tibetan, unless he be a **spiritual teacher** <note>original: *lāma*</note> and well versed in **esoteric ritual** <note>original: *Tantricism*</note>, as the translator was, the *Liberation Through Hearing in the Intermediate State* <note>original: *Bardo Thödol*</note> is almost a sealed book.

His profound **teacher-based** <note>original: *lāmaic*</note> training, his fervent faith in the higher **meditative practitioner** <note>original: *yogī*</note> teachings of The Great Perfectionist School <note>A reference to the *Dzogchen* tradition of Tibetan Buddhism</note> of **Spiritual Master** <note>original: *Guru*</note>

<summary>This page details the collaboration between the editor and Lāma Kazi Dawa-Samdup, highlighting the challenges of translating esoteric Tibetan Tantric texts into English. It emphasizes that a meaningful translation requires deep spiritual training rather than just linguistic or literal accuracy.</summary>

->### THE TRANSLATING AND EDITING<-

**The Lotus-Born** <note>original: *Padma Sambhava*, the 8th-century master who established Buddhism in Tibet</note> (he being an initiate of the semi-reformed sect known as the **Oral Lineage** <note>original: *Kargyutpa*, or Kagyu, one of the four main schools of Tibetan Buddhism</note>, founded by the great **meditation masters** <note>original: *yogīs*</note> Marpa and Milarepa), his practical knowledge of the Occult Sciences as taught to him by his late **spiritual teacher** <note>original: *Guru*</note> in Bhutan, and his marvelous command both of English and of Tibetan, lead me to think that rarely, if ever again in this century, is there likely to arise a scholar more competent to render the **Liberation Through Hearing in the Intermediate State** <note>original: *Bardo Thödol*</note> than the late **spiritual master** <note>original: *Lāma*</note> Kazi Dawa-Samdup, the actual translator. To him each reader of this book owes a debt of gratitude; for herein he has, in part, opened to the peoples of the West the treasure-house, so long tightly locked, of Tibetan Literature and **Mahayana Buddhism** <note>original: Northern Buddhism</note>.

As his close disciple for many months, I hereby formally acknowledge that debt of gratitude and respect which is ever due from the disciple to the teacher.

Though the translation was completed and revised by the translator during the year 1919, whilst he was the Head Master of the Maharaja's **Tibetan-ancestry** <note>original: *Bhutia*</note> Boarding School, chiefly for Sikkimese boys of good Tibetan ancestry, near Gangtok, Sikkim (formerly a part of Tibet), it is unfortunate for us that he is not now in this world to read the printer's proofs of it as he had hoped to do.

As to the transliterations <note>The process of representing Tibetan words using the English alphabet</note>, it may rightly be objected by philologists that they are in some instances less technically exact than they might be. The editor, however, preferring to preserve the simpler transliterations according to the old-fashioned style—to which ordinary readers are more accustomed—just as the translator dictated them to him, has left them unchanged save for the correcting of a few obvious errors which had crept in.

The editor himself cannot expect, in a book of this nature, that his own interpretations of controversial problems will meet with universal acceptance; nor can he hope to have escaped all error. He trusts, however, that critics, in recognizing the pioneer character of the work, will be prepared to concede to the editor, as to the translator, such measure of indulgence as it may perhaps seem to deserve.

# INTRODUCTION

no doubt, be interesting to all who read this book. The late <term>spiritual master</term> <note>original: *Lāma*, a title for a high-ranking Buddhist teacher</note> Kazi Dawa-Samdup—the honorific term Kazi indicating his superior social standing as a member of a landholding family of Tibetan origin settled in Sikkim—was born on the seventeenth day of June, 1868.

From December of the year 1887 and until October, 1893, as a young man whose learning the British authorities of India had already recognized, he was stationed at Buxaduar, in Bhutan, as Interpreter to the British Government. (In later years he also acted as Interpreter to the Government of Tibet.) It was at Buxaduar that he first met his <term>spiritual teacher</term> <note>Sanskrit: *guru*</note>, commonly known there as The Hermit <term>Teacher</term> Norbu (*Master-Hermit-Norbu* <note>Tibetan: *Slob-dpon-mtshams-pa-Norbu*. *Slob-dpon* means master or teacher; *mtshams-pa* refers to one who practices in a meditation retreat.</note>), a man of vast knowledge and of strict ascetical habits of life; and from him, afterwards, received the mystic initiation.

The late <term>spiritual master</term> Kazi Dawa-Samdup once confided to me that at that time he had made all necessary preparations, as a <term>disciple</term> <note>Sanskrit: *shishya*</note> on probation, to renounce the world completely; but his father, then an old man, called him home and requested him to perform the usual duties of an eldest son and marry, to perpetuate the family. The son had no option, and he married; two sons and one daughter being born to him.

In the year 1906 the <term>Great King</term> <note>Sanskrit: *Mahārāja*</note> of Sikkim appointed him Head Master of the Gangtok School, where, in the early part of the year 1919, I first met him, through a letter of introduction from Mr. S. W. Laden La, Sardar Bahadur, Chief of Police, Darjeeling, who is a well-known Buddhist Scholar of Tibetan ancestry. About a year later, in 1920, after our work together was finished, the <term>spiritual master</term> was appointed Lecturer in Tibetan to the University of Calcutta; but, very unfortunately, as is usual with peoples habituated to the high Himalayan regions, he lost his health completely in the tropical climate of Calcutta, and departed from this world on the twenty-second day of March, 1922.

As records of the <term>spiritual master's</term> ripe scholarship, there are his *English-Tibetan Dictionary*, published by the University of Calcutta in 1919, and his edition of the *Collection of the Supreme Wheel* <note>Sanskrit: *Shrīchakrasambhāra*, a major text of the Buddhist Tantras focusing on the deity Chakrasamvara.</note>

### DEATH'S MESSENGERS

> 'All they who are thoughtless and do not pay attention,
> At the time when Death's messengers appear,
> Must feel the pangs of suffering for a long time
> While inhabiting some lowly body.
> But all those good and holy people,
> At the time they see Death's messengers,
> Are not thoughtless, but pay close attention
> To what the Noble Doctrine <note>original: *Dharma*</note> says;
> And in attachment they see with fear
> The fertile source of birth and death,
> And free themselves from attachment,
> Thus extinguishing birth and death.
> They are the secure and happy ones,
> Released from all this passing show;
> Freed from all sin and fear,
> They have overcome all misery.'

->*The Numerical Discourses* <note>original: *Aṅguttara-Nikāya*</note>, iii. 35⁵ (Warren's Translation).<-

# BOOK I

## THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF THE MOMENT OF DEATH AND THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF REALITY
<note>original: *The Chikhai Bardo and the Chönyid Bardo*</note>

->HEREIN LIES THE SETTING-FACE-TO-FACE<-
<note>the "pointing-out" instruction where a teacher introduces the student to the true nature of their mind</note>
->WITH REALITY IN THE INTERMEDIATE<-
->STATE: THE GREAT LIBERATION THROUGH<-
->HEARING IN THE AFTER-DEATH PLANE,<-
->FROM ‘THE PROFOUND DOCTRINE OF THE SELF-<-
->LIBERATION OF THE MIND THROUGH MEDITA-<-
->TION UPON THE PEACEFUL AND WRATHFUL<-
->DEITIES’¹<-

---

¹ Text: <note>original: *ZAB-CHÖS ZHI-KHRO DGONGS-PA RANG-GRÖL LAS BAR-DOHI THÖS-GROL CHEN-MO CHÖS-NYID BAR-DOHI NGO-SPRÖD BZHUGS-SO*</note> From the <term>Profound Doctrine of the Self-Liberation of the Mind</term> <note>a cycle of "treasure" teachings <note>original: *terma*</note> intended to be discovered at a later time</note> through meditation upon the <term>Peaceful and Wrathful Deities</term> <note>the various benevolent and terrifying mental projections encountered after death</note>, the Great Liberation through Hearing in the Intermediate State, the Direct Introduction to the Intermediate State of Reality, is contained herein.

<summary>This page serves as the formal title and invocation for the first part of the manuscript, identifying the work as the "Great Liberation Through Hearing" (Bardo Thödol). It begins with a poetic warning about mortality to prepare the reader for the instructions on navigating the two primary stages of the afterlife: the moment of death and the dawn of reality.</summary>

BOOK I] INTRODUCTORY SECTIONS

### [THE OBEISANCES]

To the <term>Divine Body of Truth</term> <note>original: *Dharmakaya*; the ultimate, formless dimension of reality and the absolute nature of the Buddha</note>, the Incomprehensible, Boundless Light;

To the <term>Divine Body of Perfect Endowment</term> <note>original: *Sambhogakaya*; the "Body of Bliss" where enlightened beings appear in radiant forms for the sake of advanced practitioners</note>, Who are the Lotus and the Peaceful and the Wrathful Deities; <note>These deities are not external gods, but represent deified principles of our own human functions: the Peaceful deities represent the heart and feelings, while the Wrathful deities represent mental functions like reasoning and memory centered in the brain.</note>

To the <term>Lotus-born Incarnation</term>, <term>Padma Sambhava</term> <note>original: *Pédma Jungné*; the 8th-century master who established Buddhism in Tibet, regarded as a second Buddha</note>, Who is the Protector of all sentient beings;

To the <term>Teachers</term> <note>original: *Gurus*</note>, the <term>Three Bodies</term> <note>original: *Trikaya*; the three levels of enlightenment: the absolute, the celestial, and the physical</note>, obeisance.

### [THE INTRODUCTION]

This Great Doctrine of <term>Liberation by Hearing</term> <note>original: *Bardo Thödol*</note>, which conferreth spiritual freedom on devotees of ordinary wit while in the <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: *Bardo*; the state of existence between death and the next rebirth</note>, hath three divisions: the preliminaries, the subject-matter, and the conclusion.

At first, the preliminaries, <term>The Guide Series</term> <note>original: *Thrid-rim*; practical manuals that offer guidance on the spiritual path through this life and the after-death state</note>, for emancipating beings, should be mastered by practice.

### [THE TRANSFERENCE OF THE CONSCIOUSNESS-PRINCIPLE] <note>original: *Hpho* (pronounced 'Pho'); a meditative practice used to eject the mind-stream out of the body at the moment of death toward a state of enlightenment</note>

By *The Guide*, the highest intellects ought most certainly to be liberated; but should they not be liberated, then while <note>The text continues on the next page with instructions for those who require further guidance during the death process.</note>

<summary>This page opens the formal text with prayers of respect to the three levels of enlightened reality and Padma Sambhava, and introduces the 'Bardo Thödol' as a guide for liberation.</summary>

<keywords>Obeisances, Dharmakaya, Sambhogakaya, Padma Sambhava, Guru, Liberation by Hearing, Bardo, Guide Series, Transference of Consciousness, Phowa</keywords>

--- Page 171 ---
<meta>This page continues from the introductory sections of Book I, specifically transitioning from the general hierarchy of deities and the lineage of teachers to the practical application of the teachings during the actual process of dying.</meta>

->TIBETAN BOOK OF THE DEAD [BOOK I<-

In the <term>Intermediate State of the Moments of Death</term> <note>original: *Chikhai Bardo*; the first stage of the after-death state occurring at the very moment of expiration</note> they should practice the <term>Transference</term> <note>original: *Phowa*; a yogic technique for ejecting the consciousness out of the body at the moment of death to achieve a favorable rebirth or enlightenment</note>, which gives automatic liberation by one's merely remembering it.

Devotees of ordinary intelligence ought most certainly to be freed thereby; but should they not be freed, then, while in the <term>Intermediate State of Reality</term> <note>original: *Chönyi Bardo*; the stage where the consciousness experiences the radiant light and various visions</note>, they should persevere in the listening to this <term>Great Doctrine of Liberation by Hearing</term> <note>original: *Bardo Thödol*</note>.

Accordingly, the devotee should at first examine the symptoms of death as they gradually appear in his dying body, following the text *Self-Liberation by Observing the Characteristics of the Symptoms of Death.*¹ Then, when all the symptoms of death are complete, he should apply the <term>Transference</term>, which confers liberation by merely remembering the process.²

---

<note>Regarding 'transference' (of the sum-total, or aggregate, of **karmic** propensities <note>karmic: the accumulated results of one's past actions</note>, composing, or bound up with, personality and consciousness). The use of the term 'soul' being objectionable, since Buddhism, as a whole, denies the existence of a permanent, unchanging personal-consciousness entity such as the Western faiths and animistic creeds in general understand by it, the translator has avoided using it. But wherever any similar or equivalent term occurs here it should be taken to imply something akin to 'consciousness-principle' or 'compound of consciousness' as implied by the Tibetan term **Phowa**, or else as synonymous with the term 'life-flux' as used chiefly by Southern Buddhists.</note>

¹ <note>A Tibetan work of the <term>Bardo cycle</term> <note>The series of teachings related to the states between death and rebirth</note>, commonly used by <term>spiritual teachers</term> <note>original: *lāmas*</note> as supplementary to the main text. It treats the physical and mental signs of approaching death in great scientific detail.</note>

² <note>Liberation in this context does not necessarily imply, especially for the average devotee, the final state of **Enlightenment** <note>original: *Nirvāna*</note>, but chiefly a liberation of the 'life-flux' from the dying body in a way that allows for the greatest possible awareness after death and a fortunate rebirth. Yet for the very exceptional and highly efficient <term>meditative practitioner</term> <note>original: *yogī*</note>, or saint, the same secret process of **Transference** can be, according to the spiritual masters, used to prevent any break in the flow of consciousness from the moment of death to the moment of rebirth. Judging from historical manuscripts, the process is essentially one of mental discipline, and can only be used by a person trained in deep concentration to such a high degree that they have gained control over all mental and bodily functions. Merely remembering the process at the all-important moment of death—as the text implies—is, for such a practitioner, equivalent to performing the Transference itself; for once the trained mind is directed...</note>

<summary>This page explains the practice of Transference (Phowa) as a method for achieving liberation at the moment of death and introduces the importance of recognizing death's physical symptoms.</summary>
<keywords>Transference, Phowa, Intermediate State, Bardo, Liberation by Hearing, symptoms of death, consciousness-principle, life-flux, Nirvana, Yogi</keywords>

### [THE READING OF THIS LIBERATION BY HEARING]

If the <term>Transference</term> <note>original: *Phowa*; a yogic practice to eject the consciousness from the body at death</note> has been effectively employed, there is no need to read this <term>Liberation by Hearing</term> <note>original: *Thödol*</note>; but if the Transference has not been effectively employed, then this <term>Liberation by Hearing</term> is to be read, correctly and distinctly, near the dead body.

If there be no corpse, then the bed or the seat to which the deceased had been accustomed should be occupied [by the reader], who ought to expound the power of the Truth. Then, summoning the spirit [of the deceased], imagine it to be present there listening, and read.¹ During this time no relative or fond mate should be allowed to weep or to wail, as such is not good [for the deceased]; so restrain them.²

If the body be present, just when the expiration has ceased, either a <term>senior teacher</term> <note>original: *lāma*</note> [who has been as a <term>spiritual guide</term> <note>original: *guru*</note> to the deceased], or a brother in the Faith whom the deceased trusted, or a friend for whom the deceased had great affection, putting the lips close to the ear [of the body] without actually touching it,³ should read this Great <term>Liberation by Hearing</term>.

---

to the process, instantaneously, or, as the text explains, automatically, the desired result is achieved.

¹ The <term>senior teacher</term>, or reader, stationed in the house of the deceased as directed, whether the corpse be there or not, is to summon the departed one in the name of Truth, saying, ‘As the <term>Three Jewels</term> <note>original: *Trinity* or *Kon-mchog-gsum*; the Buddha, the Teachings, and the Spiritual Community</note> are true, and as the Truth proclaimed by the <term>Three Jewels</term> is true, by the power of that Truth I summon thee’. Although no corpse be at hand (as there would not be when a person had met a violent or accidental death entailing loss or destruction of the human-plane body; or when, to accord with astrological calculations, the body had been removed or disposed of immediately after death, a not uncommon event in Tibet), the spirit of the deceased, in the invisible <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: *Bardo*</note> body, must, nevertheless, be present at the reading, in order to be given the necessary guidance through the Other-world—as the Egyptian *Book of the Dead* <note>The ancient Egyptian funerary text *Pert em hru*</note> also directs (see p. 19).

² This prohibition is found in the ancient <term>Vedic traditions</term> <note>original: *Brāhmanism*</note> too.

³ According to Tibetan and <term>monastic</term> <note>original: *lāmaic*</note> belief, the body of a dying person should not be touched, so that the normal departure of the <term>consciousness-principle</term>, which should take place through the <term>opening at the crown of the head</term> <note>original: *Brāhmanic aperture*; the *Brahmarandhra* at the top of the skull</note>, is not interfered with. Otherwise, the departure may be brought about through some other bodily aperture and lead to birth in one of the non-human states. For example, it is held that if the departure is through the aperture of the ear the deceased will be obliged—before they can return to human birth—to be born in the world of the <term>celestial musicians</term> <note>original: *Gāndharvas*; spirit-beings in Indian cosmology who live on fragrance and music</note>, wherein sound, as in song and music, is the prevailing quality of existence.

### [THE PRACTICAL APPLICATION OF THIS LIBERATION BY HEARING BY THE OFFICIANT]

Now for the explaining of the <term>Liberation by Hearing</term> <note>original: *Thödol*</note> itself:
If thou canst gather together a grand offering, offer it in worship of the <term>Three Jewels</term> <note>original: "Trinity"; referring to the Buddha, the Dharma (teachings), and the Sangha (community)</note>. If such cannot be done, then arrange whatever can be gathered together as objects on which thou canst concentrate thy thoughts and mentally create as illimitable an offering as possible and worship.

Then the <term>Aspiration Prayer</term> <note>original: "Path of Good Wishes"</note> ‘Invoking the Aid of the Buddhas and Bodhisattvas’¹ should be recited seven times or thrice.

After that, the ‘Aspiration Prayer Giving Protection from Fear in the <term>Intermediate State</term>’ <note>original: *Bardo*</note>,¹ and the ‘Aspiration Prayer for Safe Delivery from the Dangerous Pitfalls of the <term>Intermediate State</term>’,¹ together with the ‘Root Words of the <term>Intermediate State</term>’,¹ are to be read distinctly and with the proper intonation.²

Then this Great <term>Liberation by Hearing</term> is to be read either seven times or thrice,³ according to the occasion. [First cometh] the <term>direct introduction</term> <note>original: "setting-face-to-face"; a technical term for pointing out the nature of the mind</note> [to the symptoms of death] as they occur during the moments of death; [second] the application of the great vivid reminder, the direct introduction to Reality while in the

---

¹ See the Appendix, pp. 197-208, where each of these chief <term>Intermediate State</term> prayers (or ‘Aspiration Prayers’) is translated.

‘Last of all, it is to be known that the prayers that follow may be conveniently said upon a sick man that <term>struggles toward</term> <note>original: "laboureth to"</note> his end. And if it is a religious person, then when the <term>convent</term> <note>original: *covent*</note> is gathered together with the striking of the table, as the custom is, then shall be said first the litany, with the psalms and <term>prayers</term> <note>original: *orisons*</note> that be used therewith. Afterward, if he still lives, let some man that is near him say the prayers that follow hereafter, as the time and opportunity will allow. And they may be often <term>repeated</term> <note>original: "rehearsed"</note> again to excite the devotion of the sick man—if he have reason and understanding with him.’

## [PART I]

# [THE <term>INTERMEDIATE STATE</term> <note>original: *Bardo*</note> OF THE MOMENTS OF DEATH]

### [INSTRUCTIONS ON THE SYMPTOMS OF DEATH, OR THE FIRST STAGE OF THE <term>STATE OF THE MOMENTS OF DEATH</term> <note>original: *CHIKHAI BARDO*</note>: THE PRIMARY <term>CLEAR LIGHT</term> <note>original: *Ösel*</note> SEEN AT THE MOMENT OF DEATH]

The first, the <term>direct introduction</term> <note>original: "setting-face-to-face"; a technical term for pointing out the nature of the mind</note> to the <term>Clear Light</term>, during the <term>Intermediate State of the Moments of Death</term>, is:
Here [some there may be] who have listened much [to religious instructions] yet not recognized; and [some] who, though recognizing, are, nevertheless, weak in familiarity. But all classes of individuals who have received the practical teachings [called] *Guides*² will, if this be applied to them, be set face to face with the fundamental <term>Clear Light</term>; and, without any <term>Intermediate State</term>, they will obtain the Unborn <term>Truth Body</term> <note>original: *Dharma-Kāya*</note>, by the Great Perpendicular Path.³

---

¹ The first <term>Intermediate State</term> is the <term>State of the Moments of Death</term> <note>original: *Chikhai Bardo*</note>; the second, the <term>State of Reality</term> <note>original: *Chönyid Bardo*</note>; the third, the <term>State of Rebirth</term> <note>original: *Sidpa Bardo*</note>. (See p. 102¹⁻⁴.)
² See p. 85⁶.
³ Text: *Yar-gyi-sang-thal-chen-po*: the 'Great Straight Upward Path'. One of the Doctrines peculiar to <term>Northern Buddhism</term> <note>a historical term for the Mahayana and Vajrayana traditions of Tibet</note> is that spiritual emancipation, even <term>Buddhahood</term> <note>the state of perfect enlightenment</note>, may be won instantaneously, without entering upon the <term>Intermediate State</term> Plane and without further suffering on the age-long pathway of normal evolution which traverses the various worlds of <term>cyclic existence</term> <note>original: *sangsāric*</note>. The doctrine underlies the whole of the <term>Liberation by Hearing in the Intermediate State</term> <note>original: *Bardo Thödol*</note>. Faith is the first step on the Secret Pathway. Then comes Illumination; and, with it, Certainty; and, when the Goal is won, Emancipation. But here again success implies very unusual proficiency in <term>meditative discipline</term> <note>original: *yoga*</note>, as well as much accumulated merit, or <term>the results of past actions</term> <note>original: *karma*</note>, on the part of the devotee. If the disciple can be made to see and to grasp the Truth as soon as the <term>spiritual teacher</term> <note>original: *guru*</note> reveals it, that is to say, if he has the power to die consciously, and at the supreme moment of quitting the body can recognize the <term>Clear Light</term> which will dawn upon him then, and become one with it, all <term>cyclic</term> <note>original: *sangsāric*</note> bonds of illusion are broken asunder immediately: the

The manner of application is:
It is best if the <term>spiritual teacher</term> <note>original: *guru*</note> from whom the deceased received guiding instructions can be had; but if the <term>teacher</term> cannot be obtained, then a brother of the Faith; or if the latter is also unobtainable, then a learned man of the same Faith; or, should all these be unobtainable, then a person who can read correctly and distinctly ought to read this many times over. Thereby [the deceased] will be put in mind of what he had [previously] heard of the <term>direct introduction</term> <note>the "setting-face-to-face" instructions intended to point out the nature of the mind</note> and will at once come to recognize that Fundamental Light and undoubtedly obtain <term>Liberation</term> <note>spiritual release from the cycle of suffering and rebirth</note>.

As regards the time for the application [of these instructions]:
When the expiration hath ceased, the <term>vital energy</term> <note>original: *rlung*; the psychic or life-force</note> will have sunk into the <term>psychic center of Wisdom</term> <note>located in the heart</note> and the <term>Knower</term> <note>original: *Shes-pa*; the mind in its perceiving or cognizing function</note> will be experiencing the <term>Clear Light</term> of the natural condition <note>original: *Spros-bral*; the primal state of mind, free from the busy thought-formation of everyday life</note>. Then, the <term>vital energy</term>, being thrown backwards and flying downwards through the <term>right and left psychic nerves</term> <note>original: *rtsa-gyas-gyon*; the lateral channels of energy known in Sanskrit as the *Piṅgalā* and *Iḍā*</note>, the <term>Intermediate State</term> momentarily dawns.

The above [directions] should be applied before [the vital energy hath] rushed into the left nerve [after first having traversed the navel nerve-center].

The Dreamer is awakened into Reality simultaneously with the mighty achievement of recognition.

<note>The "natural condition" mentioned above refers to the mind in its primal state, undisturbed by the five senses. This is comparable to the state of <term>meditative absorption</term> <note>original: *dhyāna*</note>. Consciously recognizing this "Clear Light" leads to an ecstatic state of Illumination.</note>

<page-num>91</page-num>
<header>PART I] THE DEATH PROCESS</header>

The [vital force] remains as long as there is still breath within, or roughly for the duration of a meal.¹

Then the manner of the application [of the instructions] is:

When the breathing is about to cease, it is best if the <term>Transference of Consciousness</term> <note>original: *Phowa*; a yogic practice for directing the mind out of the body at the moment of death</note> has been applied efficiently; if [the application] has been inefficient, then [address the deceased] in this way:

> O nobly-born (address the person by name), the time has now come for you to seek the Path [in reality]. Your breathing is about to cease. Your <term>spiritual teacher</term> <note>original: *guru*</note> has set you face to face before with the Clear Light; and now you are about to experience it in its Reality in the <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: *Bardo*</note>, wherein all things are like the empty and cloudless sky, and the naked, spotless intellect is like a transparent vacuum without circumference or center. At this moment, know yourself; and remain in that state. I, too, at this time, am setting you face to face.

Having read this, repeat it many times in the ear of the person dying, even before the breathing has completely stopped, so as to impress it on the mind [of the dying one].

If the breathing is about to cease, turn the dying one over on the right side, which posture is called the ‘Lying Posture of a Lion’. The throbbing of the arteries [on the right and left side of the throat] should be pressed.

If the person dying is inclined to sleep, or if a state of unconsciousness approaches, that should be prevented, and the arteries pressed gently but firmly.² By doing this, the vital force will not be able to return from the <term>central channel</term> <note>original: *dhutik*; the primary psychic nerve of the body</note> and will be sure to pass out [through the crown of the head].⁴

---

¹ When this text was first written, the measurement of time was likely still based on natural cycles rather than machines. A similar condition still exists in many parts of Tibet, where the duration of a meal is a common reference in old religious books—usually a period of about twenty to thirty minutes.

² The dying person should ideally pass away fully awake and clearly aware of the process of death; this is the reason for pressing the arteries. (See page 29.)

³ The Sanskrit term in the text is <term>dhutik</term> (pronounced *duti*), meaning “median-nerve” or “central channel,” but literally “tri-junction.” A Sanskrit dictionary defines *dhūti* as “shaking” or “moving,” which, in this context, may refer to the vibratory movement of the life-force as it travels through the central channel.—<note>Commentary by the translator Lama Kazi Dawa-Samdup.</note>

⁴ <term>Duti</term> may also mean “throwing away” or “throwing out,” referring to the exit of the consciousness from the body.

<page-num>92</page-num>
<header>THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF THE DYING [BOOK I]</header>

...through the <term>Aperture of Brahma</term> <note>original: *Brahmarandhra*; a psychic opening at the crown of the head</note>.¹ Now the actual "setting-face-to-face" instruction is to be applied.

At this moment, the first glimpsing of the <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: *Bardo*</note> of the Clear Light of Reality—which is the Infallible Mind of the <term>Body of Truth</term> <note>original: *Dharma-Kāya*; the ultimate, formless nature of enlightened mind</note>—is experienced by all sentient beings.

The interval between the end of the outward breath and the end of the final inward breath is the time during which the "vital-force" remains in the <term>central channel</term> <note>original: *median-nerve* or *avadhuti*</note>.²

Common people call this the state where the <term>consciousness-principle</term>³ has fainted away. The duration of this state is uncertain. It depends upon the person’s constitution, whether good or bad, and the state of their "nerves" and vital energy. In those who have had even a little practical experience in the firm, tranquil state of <term>meditative concentration</term> <note>original: *dhyāna*</note>, and in those who have healthy nerves, this state continues for a long time.⁴

***

<margin>Notes on the transition of consciousness</margin>

¹ See pages 18, 87, and 215. If a person is focused and alertly conscious at this crucial moment, they will realize—through the power granted by the reading of the <term>Great Liberation Through Hearing</term> <note>original: *Thödol*</note>—the importance of holding the vital-force in the central channel until it passes out through the crown of the head. —Sj. Atal Bihari Ghosh.

² After the outward breath has stopped, the "vital-force" (literally the "inner-breath") is thought to remain in the central channel as long as the heart continues to beat.

³ Text: *rnam-shes* (pronounced *nam-she*): Sanskrit: <term>consciousness</term> <note>original: *vijnāna*</note> or, preferably, <term>sentient principle</term> <note>original: *chaitanya*</note>; the "object-knowing principle."

⁴ Sometimes it may continue for seven days, but usually only for four or five days. The consciousness-principle, however—unless one is a <term>master of meditation</term> <note>original: *yogi*</note> who can induce specific states of trance—is not necessarily staying in the body all that time. Normally it leaves the body at the moment called death, holding a subtle, magnetic-like relationship with the body until the state mentioned in the text ends. Only for experts in <term>spiritual discipline</term> <note>original: *yoga*</note> would the departure of the consciousness-principle be achieved without a break in the flow of awareness—that is to say, without the "fainting" state described.

The death process is the reverse of the birth process: birth is the taking on of a body, and death is the leaving of a body; but in both cases, there is a passage from one state of consciousness into another. Just as a baby must wake up in this world and learn its nature through experience, so a person at death must wake up in the <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: *Bardo*</note> world and become familiar with its own unique conditions. The <term>Intermediate Body</term> <note>original: *Bardo* body</note>, formed of matter in an invisible or "ethereal" state, is an exact duplicate of the human body from which it separates during death. Within this body are retained the consciousness and the psychic nerve-system (the spiritual counterpart to the physical nerves of the human body). (Cf. p. 161.)

### PART 1] THE SYMPTOMS OF DEATH

In the <term>setting-face-to-face</term> <note>the ritual process of guiding the deceased's consciousness</note>, the repetition [of the address to the deceased] should be continued until a yellowish liquid begins to appear from the various openings of the body <note>often referring to the nostrils or other apertures, signaling the definitive departure of the life force</note>.

For those who have lived a harmful life, or those with damaged nerves, this state lasts only as long as it takes to snap a finger. For others, it may last as long as it takes to eat a meal.

In various *<term>Tantras</term>* <note>sacred esoteric scriptures</note>, it is said that this state of unconsciousness lasts for about three and a half days. Most other religious texts say it lasts for four days; during this entire time, the effort to "set them face-to-face" with the <term>Clear Light</term> <note>the fundamental, luminous nature of reality</note> should be maintained.

The method for applying these directions is as follows:

If the person dying is capable of recognizing the symptoms of death on their own, they should have already used their knowledge before this point. <insert>Note: The person should not only diagnose the symptoms as they appear but also recognize the Clear Light independently if possible.</insert>

If the dying person is unable to do this, then a <term>guru</term> <note>spiritual teacher</note>, a <term>shishya</term> <note>disciple or student</note>, or a close "brother in the Faith" should stay nearby. This person must clearly describe the physical symptoms of death to the dying person as they appear in order, repeatedly saying: <note>This mirrors historical European traditions like the <term>Ars Moriendi</term> (original Latin: "The Art of Dying"), which suggests having a "special friend" help the sick person with their spiritual health or "weal."</note>

"Now, the symptoms of **earth sinking into water** have arrived." <note>This is the first of three major internal sensations of dying. The three stages are: (1) Earth sinking into water: a feeling of intense pressure; (2) Water sinking into fire: a clammy coldness that turns into feverish heat; (3) Fire sinking into air: a feeling as if the body is shattering or blowing to atoms.</note>

***

<summary>This page outlines the physical signs of death and the duration of the initial "Clear Light" state, emphasizing the role of a spiritual guide in helping the deceased recognize their transition.</summary>

# BARDO OF THE DYING [BOOK I]

When all the symptoms of death are about to be completed, then urge the dying person to make this resolution, speaking in a low tone of voice in their ear:

> O child of noble birth (or, if it is a priest, O Honorable Teacher), do not let your mind be distracted.

If the person is a spiritual brother, or another person, then call them by name and say the following:

> O child of noble birth, now that what is called death has come to you, resolve thus: "O, this is now the hour of death. By taking advantage of this death, I will act for the good of all sentient beings who fill the limitless expanse of the heavens. I will obtain the state of perfect <term>Buddhahood</term> <note>a state of complete spiritual awakening and wisdom</note> by resolving on love and compassion toward them, and by directing my entire effort toward the Sole Perfection."

While shaping your thoughts in this way, especially at this time when the <term>Truth Body</term> <note>original: *Dharma-Kāya*; the ultimate, formless nature of reality</note> of Clear Light—which occurs in the state after death—can be realized for the benefit of all sentient beings, know that you are in that state. Resolve that you will obtain the best gift of the state of the <term>Great Seal</term> <note>original: *Mahāmudrā*, or "Great Symbol"; a profound meditative state of realizing the mind's true nature</note>, in which you now reside, as follows:

> "Even if I cannot realize it, yet I will recognize this <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: *Bardo*</note>, and, mastering the Great Body of Union in the <term>Intermediate State</term>, I will appear in whatever shape will benefit all beings whomsoever."

<margin>The breath comes in gasps just before the loss of consciousness. Through this, <term>spiritual teachers</term> <note>original: *lāmas*</note> who are trained in the science of death detect, one by one, the connected psychic events that lead to the release of the <term>Intermediate State</term> body from its human-world shell. The translator believed that the science of death explained in this book was discovered through the actual experience of death by learned teachers who, while dying, explained the very process of death to their students in great detail.</margin>

***

¹ In this state, realization of the Ultimate Truth is possible, provided the deceased made enough progress on the spiritual path before death. Otherwise, they cannot benefit now and must wander on into lower and lower conditions of the <term>Intermediate State</term>, as determined by <term>karma</term> <note>the law of cause and effect based on one's past actions</note>, until they are reborn.

² The Tibetan text here is unusually short. Literally translated, it says, "will appear in whatever will subdue [for helpful ends] whomsoever." To "subdue" a sentient being in the human world in this context means to take a form that appeals to that person's religious beliefs. For example, to appeal to a <term>follower of Shiva</term> <note>original: *Shaivite*</note>, the form of the god <term>Shiva</term> is taken; to a Buddhist, the form of the <term>Historical Buddha</term> <note>original: *Buddha Shakya Muni*</note>; to a Christian, the form of Jesus; to a Muslim, the form of the Prophet; and so on for other religious followers. For all types and conditions of humanity, a form appropriate to the situation is used—for example, for subduing...

### PART I: THE CLEAR LIGHT OF THE VOID

'I will serve all <term>sentient beings</term> <note>living beings capable of feeling or perception</note>, infinite in number as are the limits of the sky.'

Keeping yourself unseparated from this resolution, you should try to remember whatever devotional practices you were accustomed to performing during your lifetime.¹

In saying this, the reader shall put his lips close to the ear, and shall repeat it distinctly, clearly impressing it upon the dying person so as to prevent his mind from wandering even for a moment.

After the breathing has completely ceased, press the "nerve of sleep" <note>likely referring to the carotid arteries to manage the flow of vital energy</note> firmly; and, if you are a <term>lama</term> <note>a spiritual teacher or priest</note>, or a person higher or more learned than the deceased, impress these words upon them, thus:

> Reverend Sir, now that you are experiencing the <term>Fundamental Clear Light</term> <note>the primary, purest state of reality and consciousness</note>, try to abide in that state which you are now experiencing.

And also in the case of any other person, the reader shall set him face-to-face with the truth thus:

> O child of noble birth <note>original: "nobly-born"; a term of respect for the deceased</note> (so-and-so), listen. Now you are experiencing the Radiance of the Clear Light of Pure Reality. Recognize it. O child of noble birth, your present intellect,² in its real nature void, not formed into anything as regards characteristics or color, naturally void, is the very Reality, the All-Good.³

Your own intellect, which is now voidness, yet not to be <note>the text here describes who may perform the reading for the deceased</note> children, parents, and vice versa; for <term>disciples</term> <note>original: "shishyas"</note>, <term>teachers</term> <note>original: "gurus"</note>, and vice versa; for common people, kings or rulers; and for kings, ministers of state.

---

¹ See the following, from *The Book of the Craft of Dying*, chapter 5, *Bodleian Manuscript 423* (about the fifteenth century), Comper's edition (p. 35): 'Also, if he that shall die have long time and space to think for himself, and be not taken with hasty death, then may be read before him, by those that be about him, devout histories and devout prayers, in which he most delighted when he was in health.'

² Text: The <term>knowing faculty</term> <note>original: "Shes-rig" (pronounced She-rig)</note> is the intellect, the knowing or cognizing faculty.

³ Text: <term>The Truth-Nature of the All-Good</term> <note>original: "Chös-nyid Kün-tu-bzang-po" (pronounced Chö-nyid Küntu-zang-po)</note>, in Sanskrit: <term>The Realm of Reality, the All-Good</term> <note>original: "Dharma-Dhātu Samanta-Bhadra"</note>, the embodiment of the <term>Truth Body</term> <note>original: "Dharma-Kāya"</note>, the first state of <term>Buddhahood</term>. Our Block-Print text, in error here, gives for the All-Good (<term>All-Good Father</term> <note>original: "Kuntu-Zang-po"</note>) the name <term>All-Good Mother</term> <note>original: "Kuntu-Zang-mo"</note>. According to the Great Perfectionist School, the Father is that which appears, or phenomena; the Mother is that which is conscious of the phenomena. Again, Bliss is the Father, and the Voidness perceiving it is the Mother; the Radiance is the Father, and the Voidness perceiving it is the Mother; and, as in our text here, the intellect is the Father, the Voidness is the Mother. The repetition of 'void' is to emphasize the importance of knowing the intellect to be in reality void (or of the nature of voidness), meaning the unborn, uncreated, unshaped Primordial state.

# THE BARDO OF THE MOMENT OF DEATH [BOOK I]

...should not be regarded as a void of nothingness, but as being the <term>intellect</term> <note>original: *shes-rig*; here referring to the aspect of consciousness that perceives phenomena</note> itself—unobstructed, radiant, vibrant, and joyful. This is the very <term>pure consciousness</term> <note>original: *rig-pa*; consciousness in its most spiritual, transcendental form</note>,¹ the <term>Ever-Excellent Buddha</term> <note>original: *Kun-tu-bzang-po* (Tibetan) or *Samanta Bhadra* (Sanskrit). In this state, the observer and the observed are one, like the color of gold cannot be separated from gold itself.</note>.²

( Your own consciousness, which is not formed into any specific thing and is in reality empty, and your radiant and joyful intellect—these two—are inseparable. Their union is the <term>Body of Truth</term> <note>original: *Dharma-Kāya*; the highest state of being, a consciousness free from all mental limitations caused by contact with the physical world</note> state of Perfect Enlightenment.³ )

Your own consciousness—radiant, empty, and inseparable from the Great Body of Light—has neither birth nor death. It is the <term>Infinite Light</term> <note>original: *Amitābha*; the personification of Wisdom and the source of Life and Light</note>.⁴

Knowing this is enough. To recognize the emptiness of your own intellect as the state of a Buddha, and to see it as your own true consciousness, is to rest yourself within the <term>divine mind</term> <note>original: *dgongs-pa*</note> ⁵ of the Buddha.⁶

---

¹ <note>The original text uses *Rig-pa* for "consciousness" and *shes-rig* for "intellect." While often used interchangeably, in this deep philosophical context, *Rig-pa* is the pure, spiritual consciousness beyond the world, while *shes-rig* is the consciousness that deals with the physical world and its appearances.</note>
² <note>The name *Samanta Bhadra* means "All-Good," "Universal," or "Complete Beneficent One." This state is beyond the understanding of the normal human mind.</note>
³ <note>The union of the two aspects of mind—the pure consciousness and the perceiving intellect—symbolized as a Father and Mother, results in the "Body of Truth." This is a state of enlightenment free from the obscurations of matter.</note>
⁴ <note>The "Ever-Excellent" state and the "Infinite Light" state are essentially the same, just viewed from different angles. One emphasizes the mind of goodness, the other emphasizes the power of wisdom and light.</note>
⁵ <note>The original word *dgongs-pa* is an honorable term for "thoughts" or "mind," hence "divine mind."</note>
⁶ <note>This refers to the realization of the state beyond <term>Samsara</term> <note>the cycle of birth, death, and suffering</note>, which is the "Voidness" or the "Unbecome."</note>

PART I] LIBERATION BY RECOGNITION

Repeat this distinctly and clearly three or [even] seven times. That will recall to the mind [of the dying one] the former [i. e. when living] setting-face-to-face by the <term>spiritual teacher</term> <note>original: *guru*</note>. Secondly, it will cause the naked consciousness to be recognized as the <term>Clear Light</term> <note>original: *’od gsal*; the fundamental, radiant, and pure nature of the mind that is revealed at the moment of death</note>; and, thirdly, recognizing one's own self [thus], one becometh permanently united with the <term>Body of Truth</term> <note>original: *Dharma-Kāya*; the ultimate, formless reality or the "truth body" of a Buddha</note> and Liberation will be certain. <note>If the dying person is familiar with this state through previous meditation, they can achieve enlightenment instantly, stopping the cycle of rebirth. Most, however, lose their balance in this state—like a needle falling off a thread—due to their past habits and fears, and begin to descend into the intermediate states (Bardo).</note>

### [INSTRUCTIONS CONCERNING THE SECOND STAGE OF THE BARDO OF THE MOMENT OF DEATH: THE SECONDARY CLEAR LIGHT SEEN IMMEDIATELY AFTER DEATH]

Thus the primary Clear Light is recognized and Liberation attained. But if it be feared that the primary Clear Light hath not been recognized, then [it can certainly be assumed] there is dawning [upon the deceased] that called the secondary <note>This refers to the "Secondary Clear Light," a slightly less brilliant manifestation of reality that appears if the person fails to recognize the first, more intense "Primary Clear Light."</note>

---

<note>
The Unborn, the Unmade, the Unformed, implies Buddhahood, Perfect Enlightenment—the state of the Divine Mind of the Buddha. Compare the following passage, from <term>The Diamond Sūtra</term> <note>original: *Vajracchedikā Prajñāpāramitā Sūtra*; a central text in Mahayana Buddhism regarding the illusory nature of the world</note>, with its Chinese commentary: ‘Every form or quality of phenomena is transient and illusive. When the mind realizes that the phenomena of life are not real phenomena, the Lord Buddha may then be clearly perceived.’—(*Chinese Annotation*: ‘The spiritual Buddha must be realized within the mind, otherwise there can be no true perception of the Lord Buddha.’)
</note>

<note>
If, when dying, one be familiar with this state, in virtue of previous spiritual (or <term>meditative</term> <note>original: *yogic*</note>) training in the human world, and have power to win Buddhahood at this all-determining moment, the Wheel of Rebirth is stopped, and Liberation instantaneously achieved. But such spiritual efficiency is so very rare that the normal mental condition of the person dying is unequal to the supreme feat of holding on to the state in which the Clear Light shines; and there follows a progressive descent into lower and lower states of the <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: *Bardo*</note> existence, and then rebirth. The simile of a needle balanced and set rolling on a thread is used by the <term>spiritual masters</term> <note>original: *lāmas*</note> to elucidate this condition. So long as the needle retains its balance, it remains on the thread. Eventually, however, the law of gravitation affects it, and it falls. In the realm of the Clear Light, similarly, the mentality of a person dying momentarily enjoys a condition of balance, or perfect equilibrium, and of oneness. Owing to unfamiliarity with such a state, which is an ecstatic state of non-ego, of subliminal consciousness, the consciousness-principle of the average human being lacks the power to function in it; <term>habitual tendencies</term> <note>original: *karmic propensities*</note> becloud the consciousness-principle with thoughts of personality, of individualized being, of dualism, and, losing equilibrium, the consciousness-principle falls away from the Clear Light. It is ideation of ego, of self, which prevents the realization of <term>Nirvana</term> <note>original: *Nirvāṇa*; defined here as the ‘blowing out of the flame of selfish longing’</note>; and so the Wheel of Life continues to turn.
</note>

It introduces the transition to the "Secondary Clear Light" for those who fail to achieve liberation in the first moments after death.</summary>

98 BARDO OF THE DYING [BOOK I

The <term>Clear Light</term> <note>The fundamental, radiant nature of the mind</note>, which dawns for a period slightly longer than a meal-time <note>A traditional Tibetan measure of time, approximately twenty to thirty minutes</note> after the breath has ceased.

According to one's good or bad <term>actions</term> <note>original: *karma*</note>, the life-force flows into either the right or left energy channel and exits through any of the openings of the body. Then, a lucid state of mind arises.

Whether this state of the primary Clear Light lasts for the duration of a meal depends upon the healthy or unhealthy condition of the <term>energy channels</term> <note>original: *nadi*; the subtle pathways through which life-force moves</note> and also whether the person has previously practiced the "setting-face-to-face" instructions while alive.

When the consciousness leaves the body, it says to itself, "Am I dead, or am I not dead?" It cannot tell. It sees its relatives and connections just as it was used to seeing them before. It even hears their crying and wailing. The terrifying illusions caused by one's past actions have not yet dawned. Nor have the frightening apparitions or experiences caused by the <term>Lords of Death</term> <note>original: *Gshin-rje*; the personifications of death and judgment</note> yet arrived.

During this interval, the directions are to be applied by the <term>spiritual teacher</term> <note>original: *lāma*</note> or the person reading the text:

There are those devotees of the perfected stage and of the—

---

1. Immediately after the life-force enters the central energy channel, the dying person experiences the Clear Light in its purest form—the <term>Truth Body</term> <note>original: *Dharma-Kāya*; the ultimate, formless reality</note>—unclouded. If they are unable to hold onto that experience, they next experience the "secondary" Clear Light, having fallen into a lower state of the <term>intermediate state</term> <note>original: *Bardo*</note>, where the ultimate reality is dimmed by the stains of past actions.
2. See page 30.
3. The original text uses the term *shes-pa*, rendered here as "mind." The life-force, moving from the energy center at the navel, and the consciousness, moving from the energy center in the brain, meet in the heart center. In departing the body, normally through the <term>Crown Opening</term> <note>original: Aperture of Brāhma; the *Brahmarandhra* at the top of the skull</note>, they produce a state of intense spiritual ecstasy. The following stage is less intense. In the first stage, one experiences the Primary Clear Light; in the second stage, the Secondary Clear Light. Think of a bouncing ball: it reaches its highest point on the first bounce, and each following bounce is lower until it stops. Similarly, the consciousness is at its highest spiritual point right after leaving the body. Eventually, as the force of past actions is spent in the after-death state, the consciousness comes to rest, enters a womb, and is reborn.
4. The original text uses *Gshin-rje* (pronounced *Shin-je*), meaning "Lord of Death," but the plural form is used here to describe the many manifestations of this force.

# PART 1] RECOGNITION OF THE <term>BARDO</term> <note>The "intermediate state" between death and rebirth.</note>

visualizing stage. If it be one who was in the <term>perfected stage</term> <note>A advanced level of meditation where the practitioner has realized the underlying nature of reality.</note>, then call him thrice by name and repeat over and over again the above instructions of setting-face-to-face with the <term>Clear Light</term> <note>The fundamental, radiant nature of the mind that appears at death.</note>. So it is with those who have been taught the theory of how to act in the time of death and have not applied the theory through <term>meditative practices</term> <note>original: *yogic practices*</note>: they cannot maintain an unbroken continuity of consciousness; they grow bewildered at the changed conditions; and fail to progress or to take advantage of the opportunity offered by death, unless upheld and directed by a living teacher. Even with all that a teacher can do, they ordinarily, because of their past <term>actions</term> <note>original: *karma*</note>, fail to recognize the Bardo as such.

<meta>This page continues the instructions for guiding the deceased through the various stages of the Bardo. # BARDO OF THE DYING [BOOK I]

There may be even those who have made themselves familiar with the teachings, yet who, because of the violence of the disease causing death, may be mentally unable to withstand illusions. For such, also, this instruction is absolutely necessary.

Again [there are those] who, although previously familiar with the teachings, have become liable to pass into the miserable states of existence, owing to breach of vows or failure to perform essential obligations honestly. To them, this [instruction] is indispensable.

If the first stage of the <term>Bardo</term> <note>The "intermediate state" or "gap" between death and the next rebirth.</note> hath been taken by the forelock <note>An idiom meaning to seize an opportunity immediately.</note>, that is best. But if not, by application of this distinct recalling [to the deceased], while in the second stage of the <term>Bardo</term>, his intellect is awakened and attaineth liberation.

While on the second stage of the <term>Bardo</term>, one's body is of the nature of that called the **shining illusory-body**.¹

Not knowing whether [he be] dead or not, [a state of] lucidity cometh [to the deceased].² If the instructions be successfully applied to the deceased while he is in that state, then, by the meeting of the <term>Mother-Reality</term> and the <term>Offspring-Reality</term>,³ *karma* controlleth not.⁴ Like the sun's rays, for example, dispelling the darkness, the **Clear Light on the Path** dispelleth the power of *karma*.

---

¹ Text: <note>original: *dag-pahi-sgyu-lus* (pronounced *tag-pay-gyu-lü*)</note>: "pure (or shining) illusory body"; Sanskrit: <note>original: *māyā-rūpa*</note>: "illusory form." This is the ethereal counterpart of the physical body of the earth-plane, similar to the "astral body" described in some Western esoteric traditions.

² With the departure of the consciousness-principle from the human body there comes a psychic thrill which gives way to a state of lucidity.

³ Text: <note>original: *Chös-nyid-ma-bu*</note>; Sanskrit: <note>original: *Dharma Mātri Putra*</note>: "Mother and Offspring Reality (or Truth)." The **Offspring-Truth** is that realized in this world through practicing deep meditation (Sanskrit: <note>original: *dhyāna*</note>). The **Mother-Truth** is the Primal or Fundamental Truth, experienced only after death whilst the Knower is in the <term>Bardo</term> state of equilibrium, before <term>karmic</term> propensities <note>Habits and actions from one's past lives that influence future outcomes.</note> have erupted into activity. What a photograph is compared to the object photographed, the Offspring-Reality is to the Mother-Reality.

⁴ Literally, "*karma* is unable to turn the mouth or head," the figure implied being that of a rider controlling a horse with a bridle and bit. In the <term>Tantra of the Great Liberation</term> <note>A foundational text in some traditions regarding the soul's release from the cycle of birth and death.</note>, there is this similar passage: "The man blinded by the darkness of ignorance, the fool caught in the meshes of his actions, and the illiterate man, by listening to this Great <term>Tantra</term>, are released from the bonds of *karma*" (cf. *Tantra of the Great Liberation*, line 205, as edited by <term>Arthur Avalon</term> <note>The pseudonym of Sir John Woodroffe, a prominent scholar of Indian philosophy and Tantra.</note>, London, 1913, p. 359).

<summary>This page explains that even for those distracted by illness or bad karma, guidance during the second Bardo stage can lead to liberation. It introduces the "shining illusory-body" and the powerful union of meditative realization with primal reality.</summary>
<keywords>Bardo, illusory-body, Mother-Reality, Offspring-Reality, karma, Clear Light, meditation, liberation, Arthur Avalon</keywords>

--- Page 186 ---
<meta>Continuing from the previous page, which discussed the "Clear Light on the Path" and how it can dispel the power of past actions (karma). This page marks the transition from the state of the dying into the "second stage" where the consciousness occupies a "thought-body" and begins to witness the physical world it has left behind, before entering the more intense visionary stage of Reality.</meta>

[PART II] BARDO OF THE ILLUSIONS OF PAST ACTIONS <note>original: *karmic* illusions; "karma" refers to the law of cause and effect based on one's previous deeds.</note>

That which is called the second stage of the <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: *Bardo*</note> dawns upon the **thought-body** <note>original: *yid-kyi-lüs* (pronounced *yid-kyi-lü*); this refers to a body made of mental energy rather than physical matter, often called a "desire-body."</note>. The **Consciousness** <note>original: "The Knower"; the part of the mind that perceives and experiences.</note> hovers within those places to which its physical activities had been limited in life. If at this time this special teaching is applied effectively, then the spiritual purpose will be fulfilled; for the illusions created by past actions will not have arrived yet, and therefore, the deceased cannot be pulled back and forth or distracted from their goal of achieving **Enlightenment**.

### [PART II]

## [THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF THE EXPERIENCING OF REALITY]
<note>original: *Chönyid Bardo*</note>

### [INTRODUCTORY INSTRUCTIONS CONCERNING THE EXPERIENCING OF REALITY DURING THE THIRD STAGE OF THE INTERMEDIATE STATE, CALLED THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF REALITY, WHEN THE ILLUSIONS OF PAST ACTIONS APPEAR]
<note>original: *Chönyid Bardo* and *karmic apparitions*</note>

But even if the **Primary Clear Light** <note>The fundamental, pure nature of reality experienced at the moment of death.</note> was not recognized, liberation can still be attained if the **Clear Light** of the second stage is recognized. If liberation is not reached even then, that which is called the third stage—the **Intermediate State of Reality** <note>original: *Chönyid Bardo*</note>—begins.

In this third stage of the **Intermediate State**, the illusions born of past actions begin to shine forth. It is very important that this *Great Setting-face-to-face* <note>A ritual or instruction designed to point out the true nature of reality to the deceased.</note> of the **Intermediate State of Reality** be read: it has much power and can do much good.

Around this time, the deceased can see that their share of food is being set aside <note>A reference to the custom of placing a portion of food for the deceased during funeral rites.</note>, that their body is being stripped of its clothes <note>The preparation of the corpse for the burial shroud or cremation.</note>, and that the place where they used to sleep is being swept away <note>The cleaning of the deceased person's bed or room.</note>.

<summary>This page describes the transition from the moment of death to the "second stage" of the afterlife, where the deceased inhabits a mental body and observes their own funeral preparations before entering the visionary stage of Reality.</summary>

<keywords>Intermediate State, Bardo, thought-body, Chönyid Bardo, karmic illusions, Clear Light, liberation, funeral rites</keywords>

--- Page 187 ---
<meta>Continuing from the previous page, the consciousness has transitioned from the moment of death into a "thought-body." Having failed to recognize the Primary Clear Light of pure reality, the deceased now begins to perceive their former life from a distance before the more intense visions of the intermediate state begin.</meta>

# THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF ILLUSIONS CREATED BY ACTIONS [BOOK I]
<note>original: "Karmic Illusions"; karma refers to the law of cause and effect driven by one's past deeds.</note>

The deceased can hear all the weeping and wailing of their friends and relatives. Although they can see their loved ones and hear them calling out, the living cannot hear the deceased calling back. Consequently, the deceased departs feeling distressed.

At this time, three phenomena are experienced: sounds, lights, and rays. These experiences inspire awe, cause fright, and terrify the consciousness, leading to great exhaustion. At this precise moment, the "setting-face-to-face" instructions for the <term>Intermediate State of Reality</term> <note>original: Chönyid Bardo</note> must be applied. Call the deceased by name and explain the situation to them correctly and distinctly, as follows:

> O <term>nobly-born</term> <note>A traditional address to the deceased, acknowledging their potential for enlightenment.</note>, listen with full attention and without distraction: There are six states of the <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: Bardo</note>, which are:
> 1. The natural state of the intermediate state while in the womb;¹
> 2. The intermediate state of the dream-state;²
> 3. The intermediate state of deep meditation;³
> 4. The intermediate state of the moment of death;⁴
> 5. The intermediate state of experiencing Reality;⁵
> 6. The intermediate state of the process of returning to <term>worldly existence</term> <note>original: sangsāric; refers to Samsara, the cycle of birth, suffering, and rebirth.</note>.⁶
> These are the six.

> O nobly-born, you will experience three of these: the intermediate state of the moment of death, the intermediate state of experiencing Reality, and the intermediate state while seeking rebirth. Of these three, until yesterday, you experienced the intermediate state of the moment of death. Although the **Clear Light of Reality** dawned upon you, you were unable to hold onto it, and so you must wander here. From now on, you are going to experience the other two: the <term>Intermediate State of Reality</term> <note>original: Chönyid Bardo</note> and the <term>Intermediate State of Rebirth</term> <note>original: Sidpa Bardo</note>.

---

¹ <note>original: *Skye-gnas Bardo* (pronounced *Kye-nay Bardo*)</note>: The intermediate state or state of uncertainty regarding the place of birth or the time spent in the womb.
² <note>original: *Rmi-lam Bardo* (pronounced *Mi-lam Bardo*)</note>: The intermediate state experienced during dreams.
³ <note>original: *Ting-nge-hzin Bsam-gtan Bardo* (pronounced *Tin-ge-zin Sam-tam Bardo*)</note>: The intermediate state of deep concentration or <term>meditative absorption</term> <note>original: Dhyāna and Samādhi</note>.
⁴ <note>original: *Hchi-khahi Bardo* (pronounced *Chi-khai Bardo*)</note>: The intermediate state occurring at the moment of dying.
⁵ <note>original: *Chös-nyid Bardo* (pronounced *Chö-nyid Bardo*)</note>: The intermediate state of experiencing the fundamental nature of reality.
⁶ <note>original: *Lugs-hbyung Srid-pahi Bardo* (pronounced *Lu-jung Sid-pai Bardo*)</note>: The intermediate state of the process of returning to worldly existence; the stage where the consciousness seeks a new birth.

<summary>This page describes the confusion of the deceased as they realize they are separated from the living and introduces the six distinct "Intermediate States" (Bardos) of consciousness. The text serves as a guide to help the deceased recognize their current transition from the moment of death into the visionary realms of Reality and Rebirth.</summary>
<keywords>Intermediate State, Bardo, Reality, Rebirth, Meditation, Samsara, Clear Light, Consciousness</keywords>

<meta>Continuing from the previous page, the deceased has been introduced to the six intermediate states (Bardos). The instruction now shifts to the specific moment of entering the Intermediate State of Reality, where the deceased is urged to recognize the visions they are about to see as products of their own mind to avoid falling back into the cycle of rebirth.</meta>

## PART II] THE SECRET OF RECOGNITION

Listen now with undivided attention to what I am about to show you, and hold it fast:

O <term>nobly-born</term> <note>A traditional address to the deceased, acknowledging their inherent potential for enlightenment.</note>, that which is called death has now arrived. You are departing from this world, but you are not the only one; death comes to everyone. Do not cling to this life with fondness or weakness. Even if you cling to it out of weakness, you do not have the power to stay here. You will gain nothing more than further wandering in <term>Samsara</term> <note>original: Sangsāra; the "whirligig" or cycle of birth, suffering, and death.</note>. Do not be attached to this world; do not be weak. Remember the <term>Three Jewels</term> <note>original: Precious Trinity; refers to the Buddha, the Dharma (teachings), and the Sangha (community).</note>.

O nobly-born, whatever fear and terror may come to you in the <term>Intermediate State of Reality</term> <note>original: Chönyid Bardo</note>, do not forget these words; and, carrying their meaning in your heart, go forward. Within these words lies the vital secret of recognition:

> "Alas! Now that the uncertain experience of Reality is dawning upon me, <note>Reality is glimpsed in a state of uncertainty because the perceiver sees it through the intermediate senses of the spirit body rather than the pure, clear consciousness of the ultimate <term>Truth Body</term> (Dharma-Kāya).</note>
> May I set aside every thought of fear, terror, or awe toward the visions that appear.
> May I recognize whatever visions appear as the reflections of my own consciousness.
> May I understand them to be the natural apparitions of the intermediate state.
> At this all-important moment, when the great goal is within reach,
> May I not fear the ranks of the Peaceful and Wrathful Deities, which are but my own <term>thought-forms</term>." <note>original: rang-snang; literally "one's own mental visions."</note>

Repeat these verses clearly, and as you repeat them, remember their significance and go forward, O nobly-born. By doing this, no matter what visions of awe or terror appear, you will recognize them.

<summary>This page provides the deceased with a foundational prayer and instruction to recognize the terrifying visions of the afterlife as mere projections of their own mind. It warns against clinging to the previous life, which only leads to further wandering in the cycle of rebirth.</summary>

<keywords>Recognition, Samsara, Three Jewels, Chönyid Bardo, Reality, Thought-forms, Peaceful and Wrathful Deities</keywords>

--- Page 189 ---
<meta>Continuing from the previous page, the deceased has been introduced to the six intermediate states. The instruction now focuses on the "Intermediate State of Reality," where the deceased is urged to recognize the dazzling visions and thunderous sounds as reflections of their own consciousness to avoid rebirth.</meta>

->THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I<-

recognition is certain; and do not forget this vital, secret art lying within it.

O <term>nobly-born</term> <note>A traditional address to the deceased, acknowledging their spiritual potential.</note>, when your body and mind were separating, you must have experienced a glimpse of the <term>Pure Truth</term> <note>The fundamental, unconditioned nature of reality.</note>—subtle, sparkling, bright, dazzling, glorious, and radiantly awesome. It appears like a mirage moving across a landscape in spring-time, in one continuous stream of vibrations. Do not be daunted by it, nor terrified, nor awed. That is the radiance of your own nature. Recognize it.

From the midst of that radiance, the <term>natural sound of Reality</term> will come, reverberating like a thousand thunders sounding simultaneously. That is the natural sound of your own real self. Do not be daunted by it, nor terrified, nor awed.

The body which you have now is called the <term>thought-body of propensities</term> <note>original: *bag-chags yid-lüs*. A mental body created by one's past habits and worldly tendencies rather than physical flesh.</note>. Since you do not have a material body of flesh and blood, whatever may come—sounds, lights, or rays—they are all three unable to harm you; you are incapable of dying. It is quite sufficient for you to know that these apparitions are your own thought-forms. Recognize this to be the <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: *Bardo*.</note>.

O nobly-born, if you do not now recognize your own thought-forms, whatever meditation or devotion you may have performed while in the human world—if you have not met with this present teaching—the lights will daunt you, the sounds will awe you, and the rays will terrify you. Should you fail to know this all-important key to the teachings—not being able to recognize the sounds, lights, and rays—you will have to wander in the <term>cycle of suffering</term> <note>original: *Sangsāra*.</note>.

## [THE DAWNING OF THE PEACEFUL DEITIES, FROM THE FIRST TO THE SEVENTH DAY]

[Assuming that the deceased is bound by their past actions—as the average person is—to pass through the forty-nine days of the *Intermediate State* existence, despite the frequent instructions given, there are daily trials and dangers which they must meet and—]

<summary>The deceased is instructed to recognize the terrifying lights and thunderous sounds of the afterlife as projections of their own mind. Because they possess a "thought-body" rather than a physical one, these visions cannot cause harm, and recognizing this truth is the key to escaping the cycle of rebirth.</summary>

<keywords>Intermediate State, Bardo, thought-body, propensities, mental projections, Samsara, recognition, peaceful deities</keywords>

<meta>The deceased has just been instructed to recognize the radiant "thought-body" and the thunderous sounds of Reality as reflections of their own mind. We now move into the specific visions of the "Intermediate State of Reality," beginning with the first day after the initial period of unconsciousness.</meta>

[The instructions for how to triumph over the visions during the first seven days, when the Peaceful Deities appear, are now explained in detail. Based on the text, the first day begins from the moment the deceased is expected to realize they have died—typically about three and a half to four days after the physical body has failed.]

### [THE FIRST DAY]

> O <term>nobly-born</term> <note>A traditional address to the deceased, reminding them of their spiritual heritage and potential for enlightenment.</note>, you have been in a swoon for the last three and a half days. As soon as you recover from this state, you will wonder, ‘What has happened!’
> Act so that you recognize the <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: "Bardo". The transitional state between death and rebirth.</note>. At that time, all of <term>cyclic existence</term> <note>original: "Sangsāra". The repetitive cycle of birth, suffering, and death.</note> will be in revolution;¹ and the appearances you see then will be radiances and deities.² The whole heavens will appear deep blue.
> Then, from the Central Realm, called the <term>Spreading Forth of the Seed</term> <note>original: "Thiglé-Brdalva". Esoterically, this refers to the Realm of Reality or "Dharma-Dhātu".</note>,³ the <term>Blessed Lord Vairochana</term> <note>original: "Bhagavān Vairochana". Vairochana is the "Illuminator" or "The One who Makes Shapes Visible," the central Meditation Buddha who manifests the underlying reality of the universe.</note>⁴ will appear. He is white in color, and—

¹ This means that your experiences, which were once grounded in the human world, will now feel completely different in the Intermediate State. To someone who has just died, the world will seem to be in total confusion or "revolution." You must accustom yourself to this new state just as a newborn baby must accustom itself to our world.
² At this point, as these marvelous visions begin, you should remember that these experiences are symbolic. They are allegories for the psychic changes happening within your own consciousness after death.
³ The "Spreading forth the Seed [of all Things]" represents the fundamental source of all phenomena.
⁴ Vairochana is the Meditation Buddha of the Center. His name means "The Illuminator." He holds a wheel, which symbolizes his sovereign power over all things. His title <term>Bhagavān</term> <note>original: "Bhagavān". Meaning "The Blessed One" or "The Victorious One."</note> indicates he is a Buddha who has conquered the cycle of worldly existence.

As the central Meditation Buddha, Vairochana represents the highest path to Enlightenment. Like a central sun surrounded by the four Meditation Buddhas of the cardinal directions (who will appear on the following days), he symbolizes the One Truth. He is the source of all organic life, where all visible and invisible things are eventually absorbed.

<margin>For more on these deities, see L. A. Waddell, *The Buddhism of Tibet* (1895), and A. Getty, *The Gods of Northern Buddhism* (1914).</margin>

<summary>This page marks the beginning of the "Intermediate State of Reality," where the deceased awakens from a three-day swoon to encounter the vision of the Buddha Vairochana. It explains that the overwhelming sights and sounds are not external threats, but symbolic reflections of the deceased's own consciousness.</summary>
