# BARDO OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I]

seated upon a lion-throne, bearing an eight-spoked wheel in his hand, and embraced by the **Mother of the Space of Heaven**,¹ will manifest himself to you.

It is the **aggregate of matter** <note>The physical components of a person, known in Sanskrit as the *Rupa Skandha*.</note> resolved into its primordial state which is the blue light.²

The **Wisdom of the Realm of Reality**,³ blue in color, shining, transparent, glorious, dazzling, from the heart of **Vairochana** as the **Father-Mother**,⁴ will shoot forth and strike against you with a light so radiant that you will scarcely be able to look at it.

Along with it, there will also shine a dull white light from the **gods**,⁵ which will strike against you in your front.

Thereupon, because of the power of bad **karma**,⁶ the glorious blue light of the **Wisdom of the Realm of Reality** will produce in you fear and terror, and you will [wish to] flee from it. You will beget a fondness for the dull white light of the **gods**.

At this stage, you must not be awed by the divine blue light which will appear shining, dazzling, and glorious; and be not startled by it. That is the light of the **Tathāgata** ⁷ called the **Light of the Wisdom of the Realm of Reality**. Put your faith in it, believe in it firmly, and pray unto it, thinking in your mind that it is the light proceeding from the heart of the **Blessed Lord Vairochana** coming to receive you while in the

### PART II] LIBERATION BY THE ILLUMINATOR VAIROCHANA

dangerous **ambush** <note>original text: "hphrang" (pronounced "htang"), meaning a narrow passage or ambush.</note> of the **Intermediate State** <term>Bardo: the transitional state between death and rebirth</term>. That light is the light of the grace of **Vairochana** <term>The Illuminator, one of the five primary Buddhas representing the center of the cosmos</term>.

Do not be attracted to the dull white light of the **heavenly beings** <term>devas</term>. Do not be attached to it; do not be weak. If you are attached to it, you will wander into the abodes of the **gods** <term>devas</term> and be drawn into the whirl of the **Six Realms of Existence** <term>Lokas</term>. That is a distraction that will block you on the Path of Liberation. Do not look at it. Look instead at the bright blue light with deep faith. Put your whole thought earnestly upon **Vairochana** and repeat after me this prayer:

> 'Alas! When I wander in the **Cycle of Rebirth** <term>Samsara</term> because of intense ignorance,
> Along the radiant light-path of the **Wisdom of the Realm of Truth** <term>Dharma-Dhatu</term>
> May I be led by the **Blessed Lord** <term>Bhagavan</term> **Vairochana**,
> May the **Divine Mother of Infinite Space** be my rearguard;
> May I be led safely across the fearful ambush of the **Intermediate State** <term>Bardo</term>;
> May I be placed in the state of all-perfect Buddhahood.' <note>This prayer echoes the historical Western manual *The Craft to Know Well to Die* (original Latin: *Ars Moriendi*), which advises the dying to call upon angels for protection against the "fallacies of the adversary" and to lead the soul into their company.</note>

Praying thus, in intense and humble faith, you will merge into a halo of rainbow light in the heart of **Vairochana**, and obtain Buddhahood in the **Body of Perfect Endowment** <term>Sambhoga-Kaya</term>, in the **Central Realm of the Densely-Packed** <note>original: "Stug-po-bkod-pahi zhing-khams". This "Densely-Packed Realm" (Sanskrit: *Ghanavyuha*) is the source of all universal forces. It is also called "The Highest" (original: *Og-min*; Sanskrit: *Akanishta*), the state from which one never falls back into suffering, leading directly to final Enlightenment.</note>.

<summary>This page concludes the first day of the Bardo experience, urging the deceased to reject the tempting light of the gods and instead embrace the blinding blue light of Vairochana to achieve enlightenment.</summary>

<meta>Following the first day where the deceased encountered the white light of Vairochana, this page describes the second day of the transition through the Intermediate State (Bardo), where the focus shifts to the blue light of the Akshobhya-Vajrasattva deity and the challenge of overcoming anger.</meta>

<header>THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I]</header>

### [THE SECOND DAY]

But if, despite being shown the truth on the first day, the deceased is startled by the brilliant light and flees because of the power of their own anger or the weight of their past **actions** <term>karma: the law of cause and effect where one’s deeds determine their future</term>, or if they are overwhelmed by hallucinations, then on the Second Day, the **Indestructible Being** <term>Vajra-Sattva</term> and his gathering of deities will appear. At the same time, the consequences of one's own negative deeds that lead to the **hell realms** will also appear to receive them.

At this point, the deceased is called by name and given these instructions:

> O child of noble family, listen without being distracted. On the Second Day, the pure essence of the element of water will shine as a white light. At that time, from the deep blue Eastern Realm of Pre-eminent Happiness, the **Blessed Lord** <term>Bhagavan</term> **The Unshakeable** <note>original: "Akshobhya"</note> appearing as the **Indestructible Being** <note>original: "Vajra-Sattva"</note>, blue in color, holding a five-pointed **ritual scepter** <term>dorje: a symbolic thunderbolt representing an indestructible diamond-like reality</term> in his hand, seated upon a throne supported by elephants and embraced by the **Divine Mother** <note>original: "Māmakī"</note>, will appear before you. He is accompanied by the two **Beings of Awakening** <term>Bodhisattvas</term> **Earth-Womb** <note>original: "Kshiti-garbha"</note> and **The Loving One** <note>original: "Maitreya"</note>, along with the female attendants **The Alluring One** <note>original: "Lasema"</note> and **The Flower-Bearer** <note>original: "Pushpema"</note>. These six divine beings will appear to you.

---

¹ Text: *The Indestructible Being, the Unshakeable* <note>original: "Rdorje-sems-dpah Mi-bskyod-pa"</note>. The Unshakeable (the ‘Unagitated’ or ‘Immovable’), is the **Meditation Buddha** <term>Dhyānī Buddha</term> of the Eastern Direction. Here, as throughout the text, he appears in his **Body of Perfect Enjoyment** <term>Sambhoga-Kāya: a celestial form of a Buddha that appears to advanced practitioners</term>, which is an active, adorned manifestation. The **Indestructible Holder** <note>original: "Vajra-Dhāra"</note> is also a manifestation of the Unshakeable; both are very important deities in the Hidden Teachings.
² The **ritual scepter** <note>original: "dorje"</note> is the scepter used by Tibetan priests, modeled after the thunderbolt of the Vedic god Indra (similar to the Roman god Jupiter).
³ This name is kept in its ancient Indian form in our Tibetan text. Here the printed edition contains an error in Tibetan, calling her **She of the Buddha Eye** <note>original: "Sangs-rgyas-spyan-ma"</note>, who actually appears on the Third Day with another deity. The Mother deity mentioned here is also one of the 108 names given to **The Liberator** <note>original: "Dölma" or "Tārā"</note>, the beloved protector goddess of Tibet. In the *Collection of Universal Truths* <note>original: "Dharma Samgraha"</note> it is said that there are four great **Goddesses** <note>original: "Devīs"</note>: The Shining One, the Mother deity mentioned here, the One of Pale Garments, and the Liberator.
⁴ Text: **Earth-Womb** <note>original: "Sahi-snying-po" or "Kshitigarbha"</note>, meaning the ‘Matrix of the Earth.’
⁵ Text: **The Loving One** <note>original: "Byams-pa" or "Maitreya"</note>; the Buddha of the future who will guide humanity through the power of divine love.
⁶ **The Alluring One** and **The Flower-Bearer** are slightly altered ancient Indian names. In Tibetan, they are respectively called **The Belle** <note>original: "Sgeg-mo-ma"</note>, meaning a beautiful or playful woman, and **The Flower-Offerent** <note>original: "Me-tog-ma"</note>. The Flower-Offerent is shown holding a blossom as a personification of beauty, while the Alluring One is shown holding a mirror in a graceful pose to personify charm.

<meta>Continuing from the arrival of the deities on the Second Day of the transition, this page describes the specific visual manifestation of the white light of wisdom and the competing smoke-colored light of the hell realms. The deceased is urged to choose the blinding light of the deity over the comfortable, dull light of their own past anger.</meta>

<header>PART II] THE DAWNING OF THE INDESTRUCTIBLE BEING</header>

The aggregate of your **consciousness-principle** <term>the "knower" or mental component of being</term>,¹ being in its pure form—which is the **Mirror-like Wisdom**—will shine as a bright, radiant white light, from the heart of **The Indestructible Being** <note>original: "Vajra-Sattva"</note>, the Father-Mother <note>the deity depicted in union with his consort</note>,² with such dazzling brilliancy and transparency that you will scarcely be able to look at it, and it will strike against you. And a dull, smoke-colored light from the **Hell Realms** will shine alongside the light of the Mirror-like Wisdom and will also strike against you.

Thereupon, through the power of anger, you will become fearful and be startled by the dazzling white light and will wish to flee from it; you will feel a fondness for the dull smoke-colored light from Hell. Act then so that you will not fear that bright, dazzling, transparent white light. Know it to be Wisdom. Put your humble and earnest faith in it. That is the light of the grace of the **Blessed Lord** <note>original: "Bhagavān"</note> **The Indestructible Being**. Think, with faith, ‘I will take refuge in it’; and pray.

That is the **Blessed Lord** **The Indestructible Being** coming to receive you and to save you from the fear and terror of the **Intermediate State** <term>Bardo: the transitional state between death and rebirth</term>. Believe in it; for it is the hook of the rays of grace of the **Indestructible Being**.³

Be not fond of the dull, smoke-colored light from Hell. That is the path which opens out to receive you because of the power of accumulated negative **actions** <term>karma: the law of cause and effect</term> born from violent anger. If you are attracted by it, you will fall into the Hell-Worlds; and, falling there, you will have to endure unbearable misery, from which there is no certain time of escape. Because that light is an interruption meant to obstruct you on the **Path of Liberation**, do not look at it; and avoid anger.⁴ Be not attracted by—

<note>¹ Original Tibetan: *Rnampar-shes-pahi-phung-po* (pronounced *Nampar-she-pay-phung-po*), meaning the "aggregate of the consciousness-principle" or the Knower. A different version of the text uses *Gzugs-kyi-phung-po* (pronounced *Zu-kyi-phung-po*), which refers to the "aggregate of the body" or the physical form.</note>
<note>² Referring to the union of the male and female aspects of the deity, symbolizing the unity of wisdom and compassion.</note>
<note>³ The rays of divine grace are visualized as a hook of salvation to catch hold of the deceased and pull them away from the dangers of the Intermediate State. This is similar to how the rays of the Egyptian sun-god **Ra** <note>The ancient Egyptian deity of the sun</note> were sometimes depicted ending in hands to bless devotees, or how Christians view saving grace.</note>
<note>⁴ The deceased is here thought of, perhaps, as being able to see their living relatives and being influenced by their mourning or their own lingering attachments.</note>

It warns that anger leads to the hell realms and encourages the soul to take refuge in the light of Wisdom.</summary>

Praying thus, in intense humble faith, you will merge, in rainbow light, into the heart of the **Blessed Lord The Indestructible Being** and obtain Buddhahood in the **Body of Perfect Spiritual Enjoyment** <term>Sambhog-Kāya: the "reward body" of a Buddha, visible to highly realized beings</term>, in the Eastern Realm called Pre-eminently Happy.

### [THE THIRD DAY]

Yet, even when set face to face in this way, some persons, because of the obscurations of bad **actions and their consequences** <note>original: "karma"</note>, and from pride, although the hook of the rays of grace strikes against them, flee from it. If you are one of them, then, on the Third Day, the **Blessed Lord The Jewel-Born One** <note>original: "Ratna-Sambhava"</note>¹ and his accompanying deities, along with the light-path from the human world, will come to receive you simultaneously.

Again, calling the deceased by name, the "setting-face-to-face" instruction is as follows:

O nobly-born, listen without distraction. On the Third Day the primal form of the element earth will shine forth as a yellow light. At that time, from the Southern Realm Endowed with

But the prohibition regarding anger is essentially **related to spiritual practice** <note>original: "yogic"</note>; **spiritual practitioners** <note>original: "yogīs"</note> of all religions recognize that anger prevents spiritual progress; and it parallels the moral teaching against giving way to anger contained in the ancient Egyptian *Precepts of Ptah-hotep*.

<header>PART II] DAWNING OF THE JEWEL-BORN ONE <note>original: "RATNA-SAMBHAVA"</note></header>

Glory! The **Blessed Lord** <note>original: "Bhagavan"</note> **The Jewel-Born One** <note>original: "Ratna-Sambhava"</note>, yellow in color, bearing a jewel in his hand, seated upon a horse-throne and embraced by the **Divine Mother She of the Buddha-Eye** <note>original: "Sangyay-Chanma"</note>,¹ will shine upon you.

The two **Enlightenment-Beings** <term>Bodhisattvas: beings who have reached a high state of realization and dedicate themselves to the liberation of all</term>, **The Matrix of Space** <note>original: "Ākāsha-Garbha"</note> ² and **The All-Good One** <note>original: "Samanta-Bhadra"</note>,³ attended by the two female **Enlightenment-Beings**, **She of the Garland** <note>original: "Mahlaima"</note> and **She of the Incense** <note>original: "Dhupema"</note>,⁴—in all, six enlightened forms—will come to shine from amidst a rainbow halo of light. The **aggregate of touch** <term>aggregate: one of the five "skandhas" or components that make up a person's perceived reality; here, the physical sense of contact</term> in its primal form, as the yellow light of the **Wisdom of Equality** <term>Wisdom of Equality: the profound insight that all things and beings share the same fundamental nature</term>, dazzlingly yellow, glorified with orbs having satellite orbs of radiance, so clear and bright that the eye can scarcely look upon it, will strike against you. Side by side with it, the dull bluish-yellow light from the human world will also strike against your heart, along with the Wisdom light.

Thereupon, through the power of egotism <note>pride is the specific worldly emotion associated with this day</note>, you will feel a fear for the dazzling yellow light and will wish to flee from it. You will be fondly attracted towards the dull bluish-yellow light from the human world.

At that time do not fear that bright, dazzling-yellow, transparent light, but know it to be Wisdom; in that state, keeping your mind resigned, trust in it earnestly and humbly. If you know it to be the radiance of your own intellect—although you exert not your humility and faith and prayer to it—the Divine Body and Light will merge into you inseparably, and you will obtain **Buddhahood** <term>Buddhahood: the state of perfect enlightenment and liberation from suffering</term>.

If you do not recognize the radiance of your own intellect, think, with faith, ⁵ "It is the radiance of the grace of the...

¹ Text: *She of the Buddha-Eye* <note>original Tibetan: "Sangs-rgyas-spyan-ma", pronounced "Sang-yay Chan-ma"</note>: ‘She of the Buddha Eye (or Eyes).’
² Text: *Matrix of the Sky* <note>original Tibetan: "Nam-mkhaḥi-snying-po", pronounced "Nam-khai-nying-po"</note>: Sanskrit: *Ākāsha-Garbha*, ‘Womb (or Matrix) of the Sky’.
³ Text: *All-Good* <note>original Tibetan: "Kun-tu-bzang-po", pronounced "Küntu-zang-po"</note>: Sanskrit: *Samanta-Bhadra*, ‘All-Good’. <meta>This page concludes the instructions for the Third Day of the post-death journey, centered on the Buddha Ratna-Sambhava, and begins the transition into the Fourth Day. The text focuses on overcoming the spiritual obstacle of pride to recognize the "Wisdom of Equality."</meta>

<header>THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I]</header>

"In the **Blessed Lord Jewel-Born One** <note>original: "Bhagavān Ratna-Sambhava"</note>, I will take refuge"; and pray. This light is the hook of the rays of grace from the **Blessed Lord Jewel-Born One**; place your trust in it.

Do not be drawn to that dull bluish-yellow light coming from the human world. That is the path created by your own accumulated habits of intense pride <term>egotism: in this tradition, pride is the specific emotional "poison" that binds one to the human realm</term>, reaching out to receive you. If you are attracted to it, you will be reborn into the human world and be forced to endure the sufferings of birth, old age, sickness, and death; you will have no chance to escape the swamp of worldly existence. This light is an obstacle meant to block your path to freedom. Therefore, do not look at it. Abandon your pride and let go of your old habits; do not be attracted to that light, and do not be weak. Instead, act with trust in that brilliant, dazzling yellow light. Focus your mind entirely and one-pointedly upon the **Blessed Lord Jewel-Born One**, and offer this prayer:

> "Alas! While I wander in the **cycle of suffering** <note>original: "Sangsāra"</note> because of the power of intense pride,
> Upon the radiant light-path of the **Wisdom of Universal Equality**,
> May I be led by the **Blessed Lord Jewel-Born One**;
> May the **Divine Mother, She-of-the-Buddha-Eye** <note>original: "Buddhalochana"</note>, support me from behind;
> May I be led safely across the terrifying ambush of the **intermediate state** <note>original: "Bardo"</note>;
> And may I be established in the state of perfect and complete **Enlightenment** <note>original: "Buddhahood"</note>."

By praying this way with deep humility and faith, you will dissolve into the heart of the **Blessed Lord Jewel-Born One**, the Divine Father-Mother, within a halo of rainbow light. You will then achieve enlightenment in the **Body of Perfect Enjoyment** <term>Sambhoga-Kāya: a dimension of pure light and sound accessible to highly realized beings</term>, within the **Southern Realm of Glory** <note>original: "Shrimat"</note>.

->[THE FOURTH DAY]<-

By being guided face-to-face in this manner, no matter how weak a person's mental focus may be, there is no doubt they will gain liberation. Yet, even though they are guided so often, there are certain types of people who—having created much negative **karmic debt** <term>karma: the law of cause and effect where actions dictate future experiences</term>, or having failed to keep their spiritual vows, or simply lacking the merit for higher development—are unable to recognize the light due to their mental blockages and obscurations...

# PART II] THE DAWNING OF **AMITĀBHA** <note>The Buddha of Infinite Light</note>

**Q**

<meta>Press figure/Reference: 2089</meta>

...and evil **actions** <note>original: "karma"</note> resulting from greed and stinginess produce a terrifying fear of the spiritual **sounds** and **radiances**, causing the deceased to flee. If a person belongs to these categories, then, on the Fourth Day, the **Blessed Lord** <note>original: "Bhagavān"</note> **Infinite Light** <note>original: "Amitābha"</note> and his attending deities will appear. Simultaneously, a light-path will appear from the **Realm of Hungry Ghosts** <note>original: "Preta-loka"</note>, which is a path created by one's own history of stinginess and attachment; both will come to receive the deceased at once.

Again, the "setting-face-to-face" ceremony is performed by calling the deceased by name, as follows:

> O nobly-born, listen without distraction. On the Fourth Day, a red light—which is the pure, original form of the element of fire—will shine. At that time, from the Red Western **Realm of Happiness** <note>The pure land of Sukhāvatī</note>, the **Blessed Lord Buddha Infinite Light** <note>original: "Amitābha"</note> will appear. He is red in color, holds a lotus in his hand, and sits upon a peacock throne. He is embraced by his spiritual consort, the **Divine Mother, She-in-White-Raiment** <note>original: "Gōkarmo"</note>. He will shine upon you, together with the **Bodhisattvas** <note>Beings dedicated to universal enlightenment</note> **The Lord Who Looks Down with Compassion** <note>original: "Chenrazee" or "Avalokitēshvara"</note> and **Gentle Glory** <note>original: "Jampal" or "Mañjushrī"</note>. They are attended by the female **Bodhisattvas** **Song** <note>original: "Ghirdhima"</note> and **Light** <note>original: "Āloke"</note>. These six forms of enlightened energy will shine upon you from within a halo of rainbow light.

---

<note>1. Tibetan text: *Nang-wa-tha-yay*; Sanskrit: *Amitābha*, "Boundless (or Incomprehensible) Light." As an embodiment of one of the Buddha-attributes or Wisdoms—the **All-discriminating Wisdom**—Amitābha personifies eternal life.</note>

<note>2. Tibetan text: *Gös-dkar-mo*, "She-in-White-Raiment."</note>

<note>3. Tibetan text: *Spyan-ras-gzigs*; Sanskrit: *Avalokitēshvara*, "The Lord Who Looks Down," the embodiment of mercy or compassion. The Dalai Lamas are believed to be his human incarnations. Amitābha, who appears here with him, is his spiritual father; Amitābha's representatives on earth are the Tashi (Panchen) Lamas. He is often depicted with eleven heads and a thousand arms, with an eye in each palm—known as "The Great Compassionate One"—his many arms and eyes representing his constant search to discover and help those in distress. In China, this figure became the Great Goddess of Mercy, **Kwanyin**, often shown as a female figure holding a child.</note>

<note>4. Tibetan text: *Hjam-dpal*; Sanskrit: *Mañjushrī*, "Of Gentle Glory." A full Tibetan form is *Jam-pal-yang*; Sanskrit: *Mañjughosha*, "Glorious Gentle-Voiced One." He is the God of Mystic Wisdom, a Buddhist equivalent to the god of light and arts, commonly shown holding a flaming sword of light in his right hand and the **Book of Wisdom** <note>original: "Prajñā-Pāramitā"</note> supported by a lotus in his left.</note>

<note>5. Tibetan names *Ghir-dhi-ma* and *Aloke* are adapted from the Sanskrit *Gītā*, "Song," and *Ālokā*, "Light." In Tibetan: *Lu-ma* and *Nang-sal-ma*. **Song** is usually shown holding a lyre to symbolize music, while **Light** holds a lamp. Linked to the fire element, their symbolic color is red.</note>

# THE BARDO OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I]

The primal form of the **collection of feelings** <term>aggregate: one of the five components of human experience</term> appears as the red light of the **Wisdom of Discernment** <note>original: "All-Discriminating Wisdom"</note>. This light is glowing red, decorated with circles and smaller circles of light, bright, clear, magnificent, and blinding. Coming from the heart of the **Divine Father-Mother Infinite Light** <note>original: "Amitābha"</note>, it will strike your heart so brilliantly that you will barely be able to look at it. Do not fear it.

Along with it, a dull red light from the **Realm of Hungry Ghosts** <note>original: "Preta-loka"</note>, appearing side-by-side with the Light of Wisdom, will also shine upon you. Act so that you do not become fond of it. Let go of your attachment and your weakness for it.

At that time, under the influence of strong attachment, you will be terrified by the blinding red light and will want to run away from it. Instead, you will feel a pull toward that dull red light from the **Realm of Hungry Ghosts** <note>original: "Preta-loka"</note>.

At that time, do not be afraid of the magnificent, blinding, clear, and glowing red light. Recognize it as Wisdom. Keeping your mind in a state of calm acceptance, you will merge into it inseparably and reach the **State of Awakening** <note>original: "Buddhahood"</note>.

If you do not recognize it, think: "These are the rays of grace from the **Blessed Lord Infinite Light** <note>original: "Bhagavān Amitābha"</note>, and I will seek safety in them." Trusting humbly, pray to it. Those are the "hook-rays" of grace from the **Blessed Lord Infinite Light** <note>original: "Bhagavān Amitābha"</note>. Trust in them humbly; do not run. Even if you try to run, they will follow you, for they are part of yourself. Do not fear them. Do not be attracted to the dull red light of the **Realm of Hungry Ghosts** <note>original: "Preta-loka"</note>. That is the path of light created by your own deep habits of attachment to **worldly existence** <note>original: "sangsāric existence"</note> which has come to pull you back. If you become attached to it, you will fall into the **World of Unhappy Spirits** and suffer unbearable misery from hunger and thirst. You will have no chance of finding **Freedom** <note>original: "Liberation"</note> there. <note>Literally: "Of Freedom there will be no time." Once the deceased becomes a **hungry ghost** <note>original: "preta"</note>, or unhappy spirit, achieving the **Final Peace** <note>original: "Nirvāna"</note> after death is usually no longer possible. They must then wait for the chance to be born again in the human world once their time in the **Realm of Hungry Ghosts** <note>original: "Preta-loka"</note> has ended.</note> That dull red light is a distraction meant to block you on the Path of **Freedom** <note>original: "Liberation"</note>. Do not be attached to it, and let go of your old habits. Do not be weak. Trust in the bright, blinding red light. In the **Blessed Lord Infinite Light** <note>original: "Bhagavān Amitābha"</note>,

The text instructs the deceased to overcome their fear and attachment to achieve awakening rather than falling into a lower rebirth.</summary>

### [THE FIFTH DAY]

It is impossible that one should not be liberated by this. Yet, though thus set face to face, sentient beings—unable through long association with past tendencies to abandon those tendencies, and through bad **consequences of past actions** <note>original: "karma"</note> and jealousy, feeling awe and terror at the sounds and radiances—may find the hook-rays of grace failing to catch hold of them and wander down to the Fifth Day. [If you are such a sentient being], thereupon the **Blessed Lord Almighty Conqueror** <note>original: "Bhagavān Amogha-Siddhi"</note>,¹ with his attendant deities and the light and rays of his grace, will come to receive you. A light proceeding from the **Realm of the Jealous Gods** <note>original: "Asura-loka"</note>, produced by the evil passion of jealousy, will also come to receive you.

The setting-face-to-face at that time is, calling the deceased by name, thus:

O nobly-born, listen undistractedly. On the Fifth Day, the green light of the primal form of the **element air** will shine upon you. At that time, from the Green Northern Realm of Successful Performance of Best Actions, the **Blessed Lord Buddha Almighty Conqueror** <note>original: "Bhagavān Buddha Amogha-Siddhi"</note>, green in color, bearing a **crossed scepter** <note>original: "crossed-dorje"</note> in 

---
¹ Text: *Don-yod-grub-pa* (pronounced *Don-yöd-tub-pa*): Sanskrit *Amogha-Siddhi*: 'Almighty Conqueror.'

# BARDO OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I]

The primal form of the aggregate of volition, shining as the green light of the **All-Performing Wisdom**, dazzlingly green, transparent and radiant, glorious and terrifying, beautified with orbs surrounded by satellite orbs of radiance, issuing from the heart of the **Divine Father-Mother, the Almighty Conqueror** <note>original: "Amogha-Siddhi"</note>, green in colour, will strike against your heart [so wondrously

---

¹ That is, a **Diamond-Scepter** <note>original: "dorje"</note> with four heads, such as is depicted on the front cover of this volume. It symbolizes equilibrium, immutability, and almighty power.

² Text: *shang-shang*, refers to an order of creatures like the fabulous harpies of classical mythology, having human form from the waist upwards, and from the waist downwards the form of a bird; but whereas the Greek harpies were female, these are of both sexes. That a race of such harpies exists in the world somewhere is a popular belief among Tibetans.

³ Text: *Sgrol-ma* (pronounced *Döl-ma*): **Saviouress** <note>Sanskrit: "Tārā"</note>. She is the divine consort of the **Lord of Compassion** <note>original: "Avalokiteshvara"</note>. There are now two recognized forms of this goddess: the Green Saviouress, as worshipped in Tibet, and the White Saviouress, as worshipped chiefly in China and Mongolia. The royal Nepalese princess who became the wife of the first Buddhist king of Tibet is believed to have been an incarnation of the Green Saviouress, and his wife from the Imperial House of China an incarnation of the White Saviouress. (See p. 74.) The late teacher Kazi Dawa-Samdup noted that because Tibetans saw the likeness of Queen Victoria on English coins and recognized it as being that of the Saviouress, a belief developed during the Victorian Era that she had returned to birth to rule the world as the Great Queen of England. This belief led to a surprisingly friendly reception for British representatives in Lhasa.

⁴ Text: *Phyag-na-rdorje* (pronounced *Chag-na-dorje*): "**He Who Bears the Diamond-Scepter in Hand**" <note>Sanskrit: "Vajra-pāṇi"</note>.

⁵ Text: *Sgrib-pa-rnam-sel* (pronounced *Ḍib-pa-nam-sel*): "**Clearer of Obscurations**" <note>Sanskrit: "Dipani" or "Dipikā"</note>.

⁶ A mixed Sanskrit-Tibetan term. The corresponding Tibetan is *Dri-chha-ma* (Sanskrit: *Gandhā*), "**She Who Sprays Perfume**," one of the eight mother goddesses of the ancient Indian tradition. She is depicted holding a shell-vase of perfume.

⁷ A mixed Sanskrit-Tibetan term. The corresponding Tibetan is *Zhal-zas-ma* (pronounced *Shal-za-ma*), "**She Who Holds Sweetmeats**." Although a goddess like the Fragrant One, She of the Sacred Offerings (Sanskrit: *Naivedya*) is not always included in the formal list of eight mothers, as eight have already been named in our text. Both goddesses are green in colour, matching the light of the All-Performing Wisdom.

## PART II] LIBERATION BY THE ALMIGHTY CONQUEROR <note>original: "Amogha-Siddhi"</note>

...bright] so that you will scarcely be able to look at it. Do not fear it. That is the natural power of the wisdom of your own intellect. Rest in a state of great acceptance and balance <note>original: "resignation of impartiality"</note>.

Along with it [that is, the green light of the **Wisdom that Accomplishes All Actions** <note>original: "All-Performing Wisdom"</note>], a light of dull green color from the **Realm of the Titans** <note>original: "Asura-loka"</note>, produced by the feeling of jealousy, will shine upon you side-by-side with the Wisdom Rays. Meditate upon it with balance—with neither repulsion nor attraction. Do not be fond of it: even if your mental focus is weak, do not be fond of it.

Thereupon, through the influence of intense jealousy <note>Here, the jealousy referred to is the habitual tendencies <note>original: "karmic propensities"</note> of jealousy existing as part of the consciousness of the deceased; arising on this Fifth Day of the **Intermediate State** <note>original: "Bardo"</note>, they produce these specific visions.</note>, you will be terrified by the dazzling radiance of the green light and will want to flee from it; instead, you will develop a fondness for that dull green light of the **Realm of the Titans** <note>original: "Asura-loka"</note>. At that time, do not fear the glorious and transparent, radiant and dazzling green light, but recognize it as Wisdom; in 그 state, allow your mind to rest in calm acceptance. Or else think: "It is the hook-rays of the light of grace of the **Blessed One, the Almighty Conqueror** <note>original: "Bhagavān Amogha-Siddhi"</note>, which is the Wisdom that Accomplishes All Actions." Trust in it. Do not flee from it.

Even if you should try to flee from it, it will follow you inseparably. Do not fear it. Do not be fond of 그 dull green light of the **Realm of the Titans** <note>original: "Asura-loka"</note>. That is the path of past actions <term>karma: the law of cause and effect where previous deeds shape future experiences</term> resulting from intense jealousy, which has come to receive you. If you are attracted by it, you will fall into the **Realm of the Titans** <note>original: "Asura-loka"</note> and have to endure the unbearable miseries of constant quarreling and warfare <note>Quarrelling and warfare are the primary drives of a being born as a **Titan** <note>original: "asura"</note> in their realm.</note>. This is an interruption to obstruct your path to liberation. Do not be attracted by it. Abandon your habitual tendencies. Do not be weak. Trust in the dazzling green radiance, and putting your whole thought one-pointedly upon the **Divine Father-Mother, the Blessed One, the Almighty Conqueror** <note>original: "Bhagavān Amogha-Siddhi"</note>, pray in this way:

<summary>The deceased is instructed to embrace the dazzling green light of Amogha-Siddhi (the Wisdom that Accomplishes All Actions) and to reject the dull green light of the Titan realm, which is fueled by the emotion of jealousy.</summary>

# INTERMEDIATE STATE OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I]

> ‘Alas! when wandering in the **cyclic existence** <note>original: "Sangsāra"</note> because of the power of intense jealousy,
> On the radiant light-path of the **Wisdom that Accomplishes All Actions** <note>original: "All-Performing Wisdom"</note>
> May I be led by the **Blessed Lord, the Almighty Conqueror** <note>original: "Bhagavān Amogha-Siddhi"</note>;
> May the **Sacred Mother, the Faithful Savioress** <note>original: "Divine Mother, the Faithful Tārā"</note>, be my rear-guard;
> May I be led safely across the dangerous ambush of the **Intermediate State** <note>original: "Bardo"</note>;
> And may I be placed in the state of **Complete and Perfect Awakening** <note>original: "All-Perfect Buddhahood"</note>.’

By praying thus with intense faith and humility, you will merge into the heart of the **Divine Father and Mother** <note>representing the union of wisdom and compassion</note>, the **Blessed Lord, the Almighty Conqueror** <note>original: "Bhagavān Amogha-Siddhi"</note>, in a halo of rainbow light, and attain **Awakening in the Body of Communal Enjoyment** <note>original: "Sambhoga-Kāya"; a celestial form of a Buddha</note>, in the **Northern Realm of Accumulated Actions** <note>original: "Northern Realm of Heaped-up Good Deeds"</note>.¹

### [THE SIXTH DAY]

Being thus introduced to the truth at these various stages, even those with the weakest **karmic connections** <note>the spiritual influence of one’s past deeds</note> should have recognized the light in one or the other of them; and once one has recognized any of them, it is impossible not to be liberated. Yet, although introduced to these truths so very often in that manner, a person who has long been habituated to strong negative tendencies, or who lacks familiarity with and pure affection for **Wisdom**, may be led backwards by the power of their own evil inclinations despite these many opportunities. The hook-like rays of the light of grace may not be able to catch hold of such a person; they may still wander downwards because they develop a feeling of awe and terror toward the brilliant lights and rays.

Thereupon, all the **Divine Fathers and Mothers** of the **Five Buddha Families** <note>original: "Five Orders of Dhyānī Buddhas"</note> with their attendants will come to shine upon you simultaneously. At the same time, the softer lights proceeding from the **Six Realms of Existence** <note>original: "Six Lokas"</note> will likewise come to shine upon you all at once.

The ritual for this introduction is to call the deceased by name, as follows:

O nobly-born, until yesterday each of the **Five Buddha Families**...

<header>PART II] THE FIVE MEDITATIVE BUDDHAS <note>original: "Dhyāni Buddhas"; the five primary archetypal Buddhas representing different aspects of enlightened consciousness</note></header>

Deities had shone upon thee, one by one; and thou hadst been set face to face, but, owing to the influence of thine evil propensities, thou wert awed and terrified by them and hast remained here till now.

If thou hadst recognized the radiances of the Five Orders of Wisdom to be the emanations from thine own thought-forms, ere this thou wouldst have obtained Buddhahood in the **Celestial Body of Bliss** <note>original: "Sambhoga-Kāya"; the dimension of luminous form and clear light</note>, through having been absorbed into the halo of rainbow light in one or another of the Five Orders of Buddhas. But now look on undistractedly. Now the lights of all Five Orders, called the Lights of the Union of Four Wisdoms,¹ will come to receive thee. Act so as to know them.

O nobly-born, on this the Sixth Day, the four colours of the primal states of the four elements [water, earth, fire, air] will shine upon thee simultaneously. At that time, from the Central Realm of the Spreading Forth of Seed, the **Enlightened One** ² Vairochana, the Divine Father-Mother, with the attendant

---

¹ The philosophically descriptive Tibetan terms (which are not contained in our text) for these Four Wisdoms are: (1) **Appearance and Emptiness** <note>original: "Snang-Stong"; the understanding that phenomena appear but lack inherent existence</note>; (2) **Radiance and Emptiness** <note>original: "Gsal-Stong"; the union of clarity and the void</note>; (3) **Bliss and Emptiness** <note>original: "Bde-Stong"; the experience of great joy unified with the realization of voidness</note>; (4) **Awareness and Emptiness** <note>original: "Rig-Stong"; the fundamental nature of mind where consciousness and emptiness are one</note>.

They correspond to the four stages of **meditative concentration** <note>original: "dhyāna"</note> which arise in the same order. They probably also correspond, but in a less exact manner, to the Four Wisdoms: the Mirror-like Wisdom, the Wisdom of Equality, the All-Discriminating Wisdom, and the All-Performing Wisdom.

¹ **Meditative concentration** <note>original: "Dhyāna"</note> consists of progressive mental states: **analysis** <note>original: "vitarka"</note>, **reflection** <note>original: "vichāra"</note>, **fondness** <note>original: "prīti"</note>, **bliss** <note>original: "ānanda"</note>, and **one-pointedness** <note>original: "ekāgratā"</note>. In the first stage of meditation, the devotee asks himself, "What is this body? Is it lasting; is it the thing to be saved?" and decides that to cling to an impermanent, corruptible bodily form, such as he thereby realizes it to be, is not desirable. Similarly, having gained knowledge of the nature of Form, he analyses and reflects upon Touch, Feeling, Volition, Cognition, and Desire; and, finding that Mind is the apparent reality, arrives at ordinary concentration.

¹ In the second stage of meditation, reflection only is employed; in other words, reflection transcends the lower mental process called analysis. In the third stage, reflection gives way to a blissful state of consciousness; and this bliss, being at first apparently a physical sensation, merges into pure ecstasy, in the fourth stage. In the fifth stage, the sensation of ecstasy, although always present in a suppressed or secondary condition, gives way to complete concentration.—Lāma Kazi Dawa-Samdup.

² Heretofore each of the chief deities has been called **The Victorious** <note>original: "Bhagavān"</note>, but, herein, **The Enlightened** <note>original: "Buddha"</note> is the designation. The text contains the Tibetan word **Sang-yay** <note>original: "Sangs-rgyas"</note> = Sanskrit **Buddha**: *Sangs* = <note>The text here is incomplete but "Sangs" typically refers to being "purified" or "awakened" from the sleep of ignorance.</note>

<summary>This page describes the Sixth Day of the Bardo, where the deceased is presented with the unified lights of all Five Buddha families and the Four Wisdoms. It provides detailed scholarly commentary on the stages of meditative concentration (dhyāna) and the philosophical definitions of emptiness.</summary>

<header>PART II] CIRCLE OF PEACEFUL DEITIES <note>original: "MAṆḌALA OF PEACEFUL DEITIES"</note></header>

along with the Buddha of the **Heavenly Beings** <note>original: "Devas"</note>, named the One of Supreme Power,¹ the Buddha of the **Demigods** <note>original: "Asuras"</note>, named [He of] Strong Texture,² the Buddha of Mankind, named the Lion of the **Shakya Clan** <note>original: "Shākyas"</note>, the Buddha of the animal kingdom <note>original: "brute kingdom"</note>, named the Unshakable Lion, the Buddha of the **Hungry Ghosts** <note>original: "Pretas"</note>, named the One of Flaming Mouth, and the Buddha of the Lower World, named the King of Truth :³—[these], the Eight Father-Mother Door-keepers and the Six Teachers, the Victorious Ones—will come to shine, too. )

The All-Good Father, and the All-Good Mother,⁴ the Great Ancestors of all the Buddhas: **The Universal Goodness** <note>original: "Samanta-Bhadra"</note> [and **The Universally Good Mother** <note>original: "Samanta-Bhadrā"</note>], the Divine Father and the Divine Mother—these two, also will come to shine.

These forty-two perfectly endowed deities, issuing from within your heart, being the product of your own pure love, will come to shine. Know them.

O nobly-born, these realms have not come from somewhere outside [yourself]. They come from within the four divisions of your heart, which, including its center, make the five directions. They issue from within there, and shine upon you. The

---

to the four directions and to the **sacred circle** <note>original: "maṇḍala"</note> (or conclave of deities) to which they belong. As Tantric **Protectors of the Truth** <note>original: "Dharmapāla"; Tibetan: *Ch'os-skyon*</note> they rank with **Enlightened Beings** <note>original: "Bodhisattvas"</note>. They symbolize, too, the four tranquil or peaceful methods employed by Divine Beings for the salvation of sentient creatures (of whom mankind are the highest), which are: Compassion, Fondness, Love, and Stern Justice.

<margin>✓</margin>
¹ Text: *Dvang-po-rgya-byin*: 'Powerful One of a Hundred Sacrifices': Sanskrit: *Shata-Kratu*, a name of **Indra** <note>the King of the Gods; "[One of] Supreme Power"</note>.

<margin>✓</margin>
² Text: *Thag-bzang-ris*: '[He of] Strong Texture' (Sanskrit: *Vīrāchāra*): a name referring either to the bodily strength of, or else to the coat of mail worn by, this Lord of the **Demigod World** <note>original: "Asura-loka"</note>, the world wherein warfare is the predominant passion of existence.

³ Text: *Chös-kyi-rgyal-po*: Sanskrit: **King of Truth** <note>original: "Dharma-Rāja"</note>.

<margin>✓</margin>
⁴ Text: *Küntu-bzang-mo*: 'All-Good Mother'; Sanskrit: **The Universally Good Mother** <note>original: "Samanta-Bhadrā"</note>. The Tantric School holds that every deity, even the Supreme, has its **feminine energy** <note>original: "shakti"</note>. A few deities are, however, commonly depicted without a consort—for example, **The Gentle Glory** <note>original: "Mañjushrī"</note>, or **The Melodious Voice** <note>original: "Mañjughosha"</note>; though there may be, as in the instance of the **Perfection of Wisdom** <note>original: "Prajñā-Pāramitā"</note> (often called the Mother) which this deity holds, some symbolic representation of a feminine consort. This is, apparently, a doctrine of universal dualism <note>the idea that the universe is made of two opposing forces</note>. In the final analysis, however, all pairs of opposites being viewed as having a Single Source—in the **Emptiness of the Body of Reality** <note>original: "Voidness of the Dharma-Kāya"</note>—the apparent dualism becomes monism <note>the belief that all things are ultimately one</note>.

<summary>This page describes the final deities of the peaceful mandala, including the Six Teachers of the six realms and the primordial Father-Mother Buddhas, Samanta-Bhadra and Samanta-Bhadrā. It emphasizes that these forty-two deities are not external entities but projections emerging from the deceased person's own heart.</summary>

<header>THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I]</header>

These deities, too, do not come from somewhere else: they have existed from eternity within the faculties of your own intellect. <note>According to the secret teachings of Northern Buddhism, a human being is—as implied by the ancient mystical philosophies of Egypt and Greece—the "small world" that reflects the "great world" of the universe (original: "microcosm of the macrocosm").</note> Recognize them to be of that nature.

O nobly-born, the size of all these deities is neither large nor small, but perfectly proportioned. They possess their own ornaments, colors, sitting postures, thrones, and the specific emblems that each one holds.

These deities are arranged into groups of five pairs, and each group of five is surrounded by a five-fold circle of radiances. The male **Enlightenment-beings** <note>original: "Bodhisattvas"</note> embody the nature of the Divine Fathers, and the female **Enlightenment-beings** <note>original: "Bodhisattvas"</note> embody the nature of the Divine Mothers. All these divine assemblies will come to shine upon you in one complete **sacred circle** <note>original: "mandala"; Tibetan: "dkyil-hkhor"</note>. They are your own guardian deities. Know them to be such.

(O nobly-born, from the hearts of the Divine Fathers and Mothers of the Five Families, the united rays of light of the Four Wisdoms—extremely clear and fine, like sunbeams spun into threads—will come and shine upon you and strike against your heart.)

On that path of radiance, there will shine glorious orbs of light, blue in color and emitting rays: the **Wisdom of the Reality-Sphere** <note>original: "Dharma-Dhātu Wisdom"</note> itself. Each orb appears like an upside-down turquoise cup, surrounded by similar smaller orbs that are glorious, dazzling, radiant, and transparent. Each is made more glorious by five even smaller accompanying orbs, dotted with five starry spots of light of the same nature, leaving neither the center nor the borders of the blue light-path without the glory of these orbs and their smaller satellites.

---

<sup>1</sup> <note>The note on the microcosm of the macrocosm is integrated above.</note>

<sup>2</sup> <note>Text: *dkyil-hkhor* (pronounced *kyil-khor*): Sanskrit *maṇḍala*, meaning a sacred assembly or circle of deities.</note>

<sup>3</sup> <note>The Guardian Deities, in the final analysis, are visualizations of the person believing in them. The *Tantra of Supreme Bliss* (original: "Demchok Tantra") says that the "divine beings" (original: "Devatās") are merely symbols representing various experiences on the spiritual Path, such as helpful impulses and the stages attained through them. It states that if doubts arise regarding the divinity of these beings, one should say, "The Sky-dancer (original: "Dākini") is only the recollection of the body," and remember that the deities constitute the Path itself (see Arthur Avalon, *Tantrik Texts*, London, 1919, vii. 41).</note>

It describes the visual splendor of the blue light-path representing the Wisdom of the Reality-Sphere (Dharmadhatu).</summary>

### PART II] THE LIGHT-PATHS OF THE WISDOMS

From the heart of **Vajra-Sattva** <term>Vajrasattva: The "Diamond Being," a Buddha representing the mirror-like purity of consciousness.</term>, a white light-path of **Mirror-like Wisdom**—white, transparent, glorious, and dazzling—will shine forth. It is magnificent and awe-inspiring, decorated with radiant orbs of light that contain even smaller orbs within them, each resembling a mirror turned upside down.

From the heart of **Ratna-Sambhava** <term>Ratnasambhava: The "Jewel-Born" Buddha, representing the fundamental equality of all beings and things.</term>, the yellow light-path of the **Wisdom of Equality** will shine forth. It is beautified with yellow orbs of radiance, each like a golden cup turned upside down, surrounded by circles of smaller and even tinier orbs.

From the heart of **Amitābha** <term>Amitābha: The Buddha of "Boundless Light," who presides over the Western Pure Land.</term>, the transparent, bright red light-path of the **Discining Wisdom** <note>The wisdom that perceives the unique qualities of every individual thing while understanding their shared essence.</note> will shine forth. Upon this path are orbs like coral cups turned upside down, emitting brilliant rays of Wisdom. Each is glorified with five satellite orbs of the same nature, leaving no part of the red light-path—from its center to its edges—without these radiant decorations.

These lights will all strike against your heart at the same time. <note>Each of these mystical radiances represents a specific quality of **Enlightenment** <note>original: "Bodhic"</note> belonging to the Buddha from whom it shines. The original Tibetan text is written with such poetic fervor that the translator has provided this version to capture its beauty.</note>

O nobly-born, all of these are the radiances of your own inner mental powers shining outward. They have not come from any other place. Do not be drawn toward them out of desire; do not be weak; and do not be terrified. Instead, remain in a state of **non-thought-formation** <note>This state, achieved through **deep meditative union** <note>original: "samādhi-yoga"</note>, is considered the mind’s natural, original state. It is compared to a person floating on a river: as long as they stay passive and move with the current, the journey is smooth; but if they try to grab onto something, the peaceful flow is broken. In the same way, the act of forming thoughts interrupts the natural flow of the mind.</note>. In that state, all these forms and radiances will merge into you, and you will achieve **Buddhahood** <note>The state of full spiritual awakening and liberation from suffering.</note>.

The green light-path of the **Wisdom of Perfected Actions** <note>The wisdom that knows how to act spontaneously and effectively for the benefit of all beings.</note> will not shine upon you yet, because the power of wisdom in your own intellect has not yet been fully developed.

O nobly-born, these are known as the Lights of the Four—

---

# BARDO OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I

### THE INNER PATH AND THE SIX REALMS

Wisdoms United, [whence proceeds that] which is called the **Inner Path through the Diamond Being** <term>Vajra-Sattva: The "Diamond Being" or "Indestructible Soul," representing the fundamental purity of consciousness.</term>.^1

At that time, you must remember the teachings of the "setting-face-to-face" <note>An oral instruction given by a teacher to help a student recognize the true nature of their mind.</note> which you have had from your **spiritual teacher** <term>guru</term>. If you have remembered the meaning of those instructions, you will have recognized all these lights which have shone upon you as being the reflection of your own inner light. Having recognized them as intimate friends, you will have believed in them and understood them at this meeting, just as a son understands his mother.

And believing in the unchanging nature of the pure and holy Truth, you will have produced within you a tranquil-flowing **meditative absorption** <term>Samādhi: A state of intense concentration and mental stillness.</term>; and, having merged into the body of the perfectly evolved intellect, you will have obtained enlightenment in the **Body of Enjoyment** <term>Sambhoga-Kāya: The "Bliss Body" or "Reward Body" of a Buddha, a form of pure light and energy.</term>, from which there is no return to the cycle of suffering.

O nobly-born, along with the radiances of Wisdom, the impure illusory lights of the **Six Realms of Existence** <term>Six Lokas: The six types of rebirth in the cycle of existence (samsara).</term> will also come to shine. If it be asked, "What are they?" they are:
*   A dull white light from the **gods** <term>devas</term>,
*   A dull green light from the **demigods** <term>asuras</term>,
*   A dull yellow light from **human beings**,
*   A dull blue light from the **animals** <term>brutes</term>,
*   A dull reddish light from the **hungry ghosts** <term>pretas</term>, and
*   A dull smoke-colored light from **Hell**.^2

These six will come to shine along with the six radiances of Wisdom; at that moment, do not be afraid of them nor be attracted toward any of them, but allow yourself to rest in the state of non-thought <note>A meditative state where the mind is free from conceptual wandering and attachment.</note>.

---

^1 In the transcendental state of the Illumination of Buddhahood, on the Inner, or Secret, Path, all the Peaceful and Wrathful Deities of the greater **sacred circle** <term>maṇḍala</term> described by our text merge into the **Diamond Being** <note>Vajra-Sattva</note> in complete oneness. In total, there are one hundred and ten deities—forty-two residing in the heart-center, ten in the throat-center, and fifty-eight in the brain-center.

^2 There are irreconcilable differences between the colors assigned to these light-paths in the Wood-Block Print edition (folio 25b) and in our current manuscript. The Wood-Block Print gives them as follows: white from the gods; red from the demigods; blue from humans; green from the animals; yellow from the hungry ghosts; and smoke-colored from Hell. According to the translator, the colors should correspond to the color of the Buddha associated with each realm: gods (white), demigods (green), humans (yellow), animals (blue), hungry ghosts (red), and Hell (smoke-colored or black). Therefore, the Wood-Block Print is incorrect in all but the first and last; the manuscript is also incorrect in assigning dull blue to humans and black or smoke-colored to the animal world. On folio 23, the manuscript correctly assigns yellow to the human realm light-path. The necessary corrections have been made in this translation and in the corresponding passages in folio 46.

<summary>This page instructs the deceased to recognize the radiant wisdom lights as their own mind and warns them to avoid the six "dull" lights representing the realms of rebirth. Success in this recognition leads to enlightenment in the "Bliss Body" of a Buddha.</summary>

## PART II] LIGHT-PATHS OF THE WISDOMS

If you are frightened by the pure radiances of Wisdom and attracted by the impure lights of the **Six Realms** <term>Six Lokas: The various states of rebirth, including the realms of gods, demi-gods, humans, animals, hungry ghosts, and hell-beings.</term>, then you will take a body in any of the Six Realms and suffer the miseries of **cyclic existence** <term>Samsara: The continuous cycle of birth, death, and rebirth characterized by suffering.</term>; you will never be liberated from the **Ocean of Cyclic Existence**, where you will be whirled around and made to taste its many sufferings.

O nobly-born, if you are one who has not received the essential words of a **spiritual teacher** <term>guru</term>, you will fear the pure radiances of Wisdom and the deities who appear within them. Being thus frightened, you will be drawn toward the impure objects of cyclic existence. Do not do this. Humbly trust in the dazzling, pure radiances of Wisdom. Turn your mind toward faith and think, "The compassionate radiances of Wisdom of the Five Orders of **Buddhas**<gloss>1</gloss> have come to hold me out of their great mercy; I take refuge in them."

Do not yield to the attraction of the illusory lights of the Six Realms, but instead devote your whole mind single-pointedly toward the Divine Fathers and Mothers, the Buddhas of the Five Orders, and pray as follows:

> ->"Alas! When I wander in cyclic existence through the power<-
> ->of the five virulent poisons,<gloss>2</gloss><-
> ->On the bright radiance-path of the Four Wisdoms united,<-
> ->May I be led by the Five Victorious Conquerors,<-
> ->May the Five Orders of Divine Mothers be my rear-<-
> ->guard;<-
> ->May I be rescued from the impure light-paths of the Six<-
> ->Realms;<-
> ->And, being saved from the ambushes of the terrifying **Intermediate State** <term>Bardo</term>,<-
> ->May I be placed within the five pure Divine Realms."<-

By praying in this way, a person recognizes their own **inner light**;<gloss>3</gloss>

---

<gloss>1</gloss> The original Tibetan term is *Bde-var-gshegs-pa* (pronounced *De-war-sheg-pa*). This corresponds to the Sanskrit term *Sugata*, which literally means "Those who have passed into Happiness" (or attained **Nirvana** <term>the state of liberation from suffering</term>)—that is, the Buddhas. <note>original: *Bde-var-gshegs-pa* / *Sugata*</note>
<gloss>2</gloss> The five virulent poisons are mental afflictions which, like drugs, enslave and bind humanity to the sufferings of existence within the Six Realms. They are: lust, hatred, stupidity, pride (or egoism), and jealousy.
<gloss>3</gloss> The original Tibetan text uses *rang* ("self") and *sNang* ("light"), meaning "self-light" or "inner-light." These are thoughts or ideas appearing within the radiance of one's own consciousness. The Intermediate State (or *Bardo*) is the dream-like state after death that follows the waking state of life on earth. <note>original: *rang-sNang*</note>

Instead, it provides a specific prayer to help the soul recognize the brilliant lights of the Five Buddha families as their own inner nature, leading to liberation.</summary>

# THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I]

...and, merging one's self into it in perfect unity, the state of a **Buddha** <gloss>An enlightened being</gloss> is attained. Through humble faith, even an ordinary devotee can come to know their true nature and obtain **Liberation** <gloss>freedom from the cycle of rebirth</gloss>. Even the most lowly person, by the power of sincere prayer, can close the doors of the **Six Realms** <term>Six Lokas: The six states of existence (gods, demi-gods, humans, animals, hungry ghosts, and hell-beings) in which one can be reborn.</term> and, by understanding the real meaning of the **Four Wisdoms** united, obtain Buddhahood by the direct pathway through the **Diamond Being** <note>original: "Vajra-Sattva"</note>.¹

Thus, by being set face-to-face with the truth in this detailed manner, those who are destined to be freed will come to recognize **the Truth** <gloss>The reality of the mind's nature</gloss>;² thereby many will attain Liberation.

The worst of the worst—those with heavy negative **karmic** <term>Karma: The spiritual law of cause and effect resulting from one's past actions.</term> burdens who have not the least interest in any religion, and some who have failed in their sacred vows—will fail to recognize the truth, even though it is shown to them. Through the power of their own illusions, they will stray downward into lower states of existence.

### [THE SEVENTH DAY]

On the Seventh Day, the **Knowledge-Holding Deities** <term>Vidyadharas: Enlightened beings who "hold" or embody spiritual wisdom and power.</term>, coming from the holy paradise realms, appear to receive you. Simultaneously, the pathway to the **animal world** <note>original: "brute world"</note>, produced by the obscuring passion of stupidity, also appears to receive you.³ The instruction at that time is given by calling the deceased by name, as follows:

---

The aim of the **Liberation by Hearing** <note>original: "Bardo Thödol"</note> teaching is to awaken the Dreamer to Reality—to a state of consciousness beyond this world, to the destruction of all bonds of **cyclic existence** <note>original: "sangsāric"</note>, and to Perfect Enlightenment or Buddhahood.

¹ The **Diamond Being** <note>original: "Vajra-Sattva"</note>, as a symbolic deity and a reflection of the **Immovable Buddha** <note>original: "Akṣhobhya"</note>, is visualized in Tibetan rituals as being internally empty or transparent. As such, he represents the **Void** <gloss>Sunyata: The ultimate nature of reality, which is empty of independent existence</gloss>. There are many complex teachings on this subject. Through the Diamond Being, there is a certain path to Liberation, as he embodies all one-hundred and ten deities that make up the **Sacred Circle** <note>original: "maṇḍala"</note> of the Peaceful and Wrathful Ones. To travel this path successfully, the student must be guided by a master or **High Priest** <note>original: "Hierophant"</note>.

² This Truth is the realization that there is no reality behind any of the visions seen in the **Intermediate State** <note>original: "Bardo"</note>; they are merely illusions created by one's own mind and past experiences in the world. Recognizing this automatically brings Liberation.

³ Just as the physical atoms of a dead body eventually separate—some becoming gases, some fluids, and some solids—the "psychic atoms" or mental habits of the deceased also begin to disperse on the after-death plane. Each habit or tendency is drawn by its own nature to an environment that matches it. Here, the text suggests that the energy of "stupidity" or "ignorance" naturally gravitates toward the animal kingdom, eventually becoming part of an animal's consciousness.

<summary>This page describes how faith and prayer can lead to liberation even for ordinary people, while those with heavy negative karma may fail to recognize the truth. It introduces the Seventh Day of the Bardo, where the Knowledge-Holding Deities appear alongside the tempting path to the animal realm.</summary>

## ## PART II | KNOWLEDGE-HOLDING DEITIES

O child of noble birth, listen without distraction. On the Seventh Day, the multi-colored radiance of your purified instincts will shine forth. At the same time, the **Knowledge-Holding Deities** <term>Vidyadharas: Enlightened beings who "hold" or embody spiritual wisdom and power.</term>,¹ coming from the holy paradise realms, will arrive to receive you.

From the center of the Circle <gloss>or Mandala</gloss>, surrounded by a halo of rainbow light, the supreme **Knowledge-Holder** <note>original: "Rig-ḥdzin"</note>, the **Lotus Lord of Dance**, will appear. He is the Supreme Knowledge-Holder who brings karmic seeds to ripeness. He shines with all the five colors and is embraced by his Divine Mother, the **Red Sky-goer** <note>original: "Dakini"</note>.² He holds a crescent-shaped knife and a human skull filled with blood,³ dancing and performing the **ritual gesture** <note>original: "mudra"</note> of fascination.⁴ With his right hand held high, he will shine brilliantly.

To the east of that Circle, the deity called the **Earth-Abiding Knowledge-Holder** will appear. He is white in color with a radiant, smiling face, and is embraced by the **White Sky-goer**, the Divine Mother. He also holds a crescent knife and a skull filled with blood, dancing and performing the ritual gesture of fascination with his right hand held high.

To the south of that Circle, the Knowledge-Holding Deity called **He Who Has Power Over the Duration of Life** will appear. He is yellow in color, smiling and radiant, and is embraced by the **Yellow Sky-goer**, the Divine Mother. He holds a crescent knife and a skull—

<margin>¹ The Tibetan term is *Rig-ḥdzin* <note>original: "Rig-ḥdzin"</note>, which means "possessing (or holding) knowledge." These deities are specific to **Tantric** <term>Tantra: An esoteric system of Buddhist practice using rituals, visualization, and yoga to reach enlightenment.</term> traditions.</margin>

<margin>² The **Sky-goers** <note>original: "Mkhah-ḥgro-ma" or "Dakini"</note> are fairy-like goddesses who possess supernatural powers for either good or evil. They are essential to **Tantric** rituals and are invoked in most major ceremonies of Northern **Buddhism**.</margin>

<margin>³ In a symbolic sense, the **human skull** and the **blood** filling it represent the renunciation of worldly life. It signifies giving up the ego and the **cycle of suffering** <note>original: "Saṃsāra"</note>. In the rituals of Tibetan Buddhism, there are parallels between this symbolic blood (often represented by a red liquid) and the **wine** used as the blood of Christ in the **Christian Communion**.</margin>

<margin>⁴ A **ritual gesture** <note>original: "mudrā"</note> is a symbolic sign made with the hands, fingers, or the entire body. Some are used as secret signs of recognition, much like a **Masonic handshake**. Others, used by practitioners of yoga, help to direct the spiritual energy or "life-currents" of the body. The **gesture of fascination** is one of these; it is made with the right hand by touching the thumb to the middle finger, while the index and pinky fingers are held upright and the ring finger is folded into the palm.</margin>

# BARDO OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I]

...[filled] with blood, dancing and making the **ritual gesture** <note>original: "mudrā"</note> of fascination, [with his right hand held] aloft, will come to shine.

To the west of that Circle, the deity called the **Knowledge-Holder of the Great Seal** <term>Mahamudra: The "Great Symbol" or "Great Seal," representing a state of direct realization of the nature of mind.</term>, red of color, smiling and radiant, embraced by the **Red Sky-goer** <note>original: "Dakini"</note>, the [Divine] Mother, [he] holding a crescent-knife and a skull [filled] with blood, dancing and making the **ritual gesture** <note>original: "mudrā"</note> of fascination, [with his right hand held] aloft, will come to shine.

To the north of that Circle, the deity called the **Spontaneously Arisen Knowledge-Holder**, green of color, with a half-angry, half-smiling radiant countenance, embraced by the **Green Sky-goer** <note>original: "Dakini"</note>, the [Divine] Mother, [he] holding a crescent-knife and a skull [filled] with blood, dancing and making the **ritual gesture** <note>original: "mudrā"</note> of fascination, [with his right hand held] aloft, will come to shine.

In the Outer Circle, round about these Knowledge-Holders, innumerable bands of **Sky-goers** <note>original: "Dakinis," feminine embodiments of enlightened energy</note>,—**Sky-goers** of the eight places of cremation <note>These refer to traditional Indian burial grounds where practitioners meditate on impermanence.</note>, **Sky-goers** of the four classes, **Sky-goers** of the three abodes <note>The three abodes refer to the energy centers of the heart, throat, and brain.</note>, **Sky-goers** of the thirty holy-places and of the twenty-four places of pilgrimage,—heroes, heroines, celestial warriors, and faith-protecting deities, male and female, each bedecked with the six bone-ornaments, having drums and thigh-bone trumpets, skull-timbrels, banners of gigantic **demon**[-like] hides <note>original: "rakshasas," a class of powerful, sometimes malevolent spirits</note>, **demon**-hide canopies, **demon**-hide bannerettes, fumes of human-fat incense, and innumerable [other] kinds of musical instruments, filling [with music] the whole world-systems and causing them to vibrate, to quake and tremble with sounds so mighty as to daze one's brain, and dancing various measures, will come to receive the faithful and punish the unfaithful <note>In the context of the Bardo, "punishing" refers to the terrifying personification of one's own ignored conscience or failed spiritual commitments.</note>.

---

<margin>
1. See p. 135¹.
2. Herein the **Sky-goers** are represented like various orders of fairy-like beings, some dwelling in one place, some in another. The eight places of cremation are the eight known to Hindu mythology; the three abodes are the heart-center, the throat-center, and the brain-center, over which, from an internal perspective, certain **Sky-goers** (as the personification of the psychic forces resident in each center) preside, just as others preside over the holy-places and places of pilgrimage.
3. That is, hides of **giant spirits** <note>original: "rakshasas"</note>, an order of giant demoniacal beings having human form and possessed of certain **spiritual accomplishments** <note>original: "siddhis," or supernormal powers</note>.
4. Tibetan priests <note>original: "lamas"</note>, in chanting their rituals, employ seven (or eight) sorts of musical instruments to evoke these states.
</margin>

## PART II: WISDOM RADIANCES

O nobly-born, five-colored radiances of the **Wisdom of the Simultaneously-Born** <term>Sahaja-jnana: A state of enlightenment or "Wisdom" that exists naturally and is "born" at the same moment one recognizes their true nature.</term>, which are the purified tendencies of the mind, vibrating and dazzling like colored threads, flashing, radiant, and transparent, glorious and awe-inspiring, will issue from the hearts of the five chief **Knowledge-Holding Deities** <term>Vidyadharas: Beings of great spiritual realization who "hold" or embody transformative wisdom.</term> and strike against your heart. These lights are so bright that the eye cannot bear to look upon them.

At the same time, a dull blue light from the **brute world** <note>This refers to the Animal Realm, one of the six states of existence in Buddhist cosmology, characterized by instinct and lack of self-awareness.</note> will come to shine along with these Radiances of Wisdom. Then, through the influence of the illusions of your past habits, you will feel afraid of the radiance of the five colors; and, wishing to flee from it, you will feel attracted toward the dull light from the animal world. Thereupon, do not be afraid of that brilliant radiance of five colors, nor be terrified; but recognize this Wisdom to be your own inner nature.

Within those radiances, the natural sound of the **Truth** <note>original: "Dharma"</note> will reverberate like a thousand thunders. The sound will come with a rolling reverberation, amidst which will be heard the cry, ‘Slay! Slay!’ and awe-inspiring *mantras* <term>Mantra: Sacred syllables or phrases used to focus the mind and invoke spiritual power; the text here notes these are explained further in the book's appendix.</term>. Fear not. Flee not. Be not terrified. Know these sounds to be the expressions of the intellectual faculties of your own inner light.

---

### TIBETAN SACRED INSTRUMENTS
The sounds described above are represented in ritual by musical instruments: big drums, cymbals (commonly of brass), conch-shells, bells (similar to the handbells used in the **Christian Mass Service** <note>A comparison made by the original translator to help Western readers visualize the ritual.</note>), timbrels, small clarionets (sounding like **Highland bagpipes**), big trumpets, and human thigh-bone trumpets.

Although the combined sounds of these instruments are far from being melodious, the *lāmas* <term>Lama: A spiritual teacher or monk in the Tibetan tradition.</term> maintain that they psychically produce in the devotee an attitude of deep veneration and faith. This is because they are the earthly counterparts of the natural sounds which one’s own body is heard producing when the fingers are put in the ears to shut out external sounds. 

When stopping the ears in this way, one hears:
*   A thudding sound, like that of a big drum being beaten; 
*   A clashing sound, as of cymbals; 
*   A soughing sound, as of a wind moving through a forest—similar to when a conch-shell is blown; 
*   A ringing as of bells; 
*   A sharp tapping sound, as when a timbrel is used; 
*   A moaning sound, like that of a clarionet; 
*   A bass moaning sound, as if made with a big trumpet; 
*   And a shriller sound, as of a thigh-bone trumpet.

Not only is this interesting as a theory of Tibetan sacred music, but it gives the clue to the esoteric interpretation of the symbolical "natural sounds of Truth" referred to in the text. These sounds are said to proceed from the intellectual faculties within the human mentality itself.

<summary>This page describes the blinding five-colored lights and thunderous sounds of the Knowledge-Holding Deities, explaining that these experiences are actually reflections of the deceased's own mind and should not be feared.</summary>

# BARDO OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I]

Do not be attracted toward the dull blue light of the **animal world** <note>original: "brute-world." This refers to the Animal Realm, one of the six states of existence, characterized by instinct and lack of self-awareness.</note>; do not be weak. If you are attracted to it, you will fall into the animal world where stupidity is the dominant state. There, you will suffer the endless miseries of slavery, being unable to speak, and living in a dull, mindless state <note>Compare with page 126, which describes similar suffering in lower realms.</note>; and it will be a very long time before you can escape. Do not be attracted to it. Instead, put your faith in the bright, dazzling, five-colored radiance. Direct your mind with total focus toward the deities, the **Knowledge-Holding Conquerors** <term>Vidyadharas: Advanced spiritual beings who embody and "hold" transformative wisdom.</term>. Think with single-minded focus: “These Knowledge-Holding Deities, the Heroes, and the **Sky-Goers** <term>Dakinis: Feminine spirits or goddesses who represent the movement of spiritual energy and wisdom.</term> have come from the holy paradise realms to receive me; I pray to them all. Up to this day, although the five groups of Buddhas of the past, present, and future have all shone the rays of their grace and compassion upon me, I have not yet been rescued. Alas, for a being like me! May the Knowledge-Holding Deities not let me fall further down than this, but catch me with the hook of their compassion and lead me to the holy paradises.”

Thinking in that manner, with total focus, pray in this way:

> “O Knowledge-Holding Deities, please listen to me;
> Lead me on the Path through your great love.
> When I am wandering in the **cycle of rebirth** <term>Samsara: The repetitive cycle of birth, death, and existence driven by karma.</term> because of my strong past habits,
> On the bright light-path of **Innate Wisdom** <term>Sahaja-jnana: A state of enlightenment that is "born together" with the realization of one's true nature.</term>,
> May the bands of Heroes, the Knowledge-Holders, lead me;
> May the groups of the Mothers, the **Sky-Goers**, be my rear-guard;
> May they save me from the terrifying ambushes of the **intermediate state** <term>Bardo: The transitional state between death and the next rebirth.</term>,
> And place me in the pure paradise realms.”

By praying this way with deep faith and humility, there is no doubt that you will be born within the pure paradise realms <note>At this point, the deceased has fallen to lower stages of the Bardo. They now look toward the "heavenly worlds" (states of existence within the cycle of rebirth) as a place of refuge rather than seeking **Nirvana** (liberation from all rebirth). While liberation is technically possible at any time, it is difficult for an ordinary person with limited spiritual merit. Therefore, the priest or reader helps the deceased aim for the best possible rebirth available to them.</note>, after...

<summary>This page warns the deceased against falling into the animal realm and provides a prayer to the Knowledge-Holding Deities (Vidyadharas) for guidance toward a favorable rebirth in a paradise realm.</summary>

being merged, in rainbow-light, into the heart of the **Knowledge-Holding Deities** <term>Vidyadharas: Advanced spiritual beings who embody and "hold" transformative wisdom.</term>.

All the scholar <note>original: "pandit"</note> classes, too, coming to recognize at this stage, obtain liberation; even those of evil tendencies are sure to be liberated here.

Here ends the part of the Great **Liberation through Hearing** <note>original: "Thödol"</note> concerned with the introduction to the **Peaceful Deities** of the **Intermediate State of Reality** <note>original: "Chönyid Bardo"</note> and the introduction to the **Clear Light of the Intermediate State of the Moment of Death** <note>original: "Chikhai Bardo"</note>.

## [THE DAWNING OF THE WRATHFUL DEITIES, FROM THE EIGHTH TO THE FOURTEENTH DAY]

### [INTRODUCTION]

Now the manner of the dawning of the **Wrathful Deities** is to be shown.

In the above **Intermediate State** <note>original: "Bardo"</note> of the Peaceful Deities, there were seven stages of ambush. The introduction at each stage should have caused the deceased to recognize the truth at one or another stage and to have been liberated.

Multitudes will be liberated by that recognition; and although many obtain liberation in that manner, because the number of sentient beings is so vast, their **karmic actions** <term>Karma: The law of cause and effect, where one's past intentions and actions shape their future reality.</term> so powerful, their mental obstructions so dense, and their habits so long-standing, the **Wheel of Ignorance and Illusion** neither slows down nor stops. Although everyone is introduced to the truth in such detail, a vast majority of people continue to wander downward without being liberated.

Therefore, after the peaceful and knowledge-holding deities (who come to welcome you) have finished appearing, the fifty-eight flame-haloed, wrathful, blood-drinking deities begin to appear. These are actually the same peaceful deities you saw before, but appearing in a different form—corresponding to the specific energy center <note>original: "psychic-centre"</note> of the **Intermediate State body** <term>Bardo-body: The subtle, mental body a person inhabits after death before their next rebirth.</term> from which they emerge; nevertheless, they will not look like the peaceful ones.¹

---

¹ Up to this time, the fifty-two Peaceful and Knowledge-Holding Deities—emanations from the heart and throat energy centers of the **Intermediate State body** of the deceased—have appeared. The Wrathful Deities now about to appear issue from
