# THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I]

This is the **Intermediate State** <note>original: "Bardo"</note> of the **Wrathful Deities**. Because they appear in forms that provoke intense fear, terror, and awe, recognizing them as reflections of one's own mind becomes much more difficult. The mind, unable to remain independent or stable, passes from one state of fainting into a recurring cycle of loss of consciousness. [Yet], if a person can manage even a small degree of recognition, it is actually easier to find liberation at this stage. If you ask why? It is because the dawning of these intense radiances—which produce that fear, terror, and awe—forces the mind to become sharply alert and focused on a single point. That is the reason.

If a person does not encounter this specific teaching at this stage, all their previous religious study—even if it is as vast as an ocean—will be of no use. Even high-ranking **monks** <term>Bhikkhus: Ordained Buddhist monks who follow a strict code of discipline.</term> who hold their vows perfectly and scholars of complex philosophy <note>original: "doctors in metaphysical discourses"</note> can lose their way at this point. If they fail to recognize the nature of these visions, they will continue to wander in the **Cycle of Rebirth and Suffering** <term>Samsara: The beginningless cycle of birth, death, and rebirth driven by karma and ignorance.</term>.

As for ordinary people who have no spiritual practice, there is no need to even mention their struggle! Driven by fear, terror, and awe, they try to flee from the visions, only to fall over symbolic precipices into unhappy realms where they suffer. However, even the most humble practitioner of the **Path of Sacred Sound** <term>Mantrayana: A branch of Buddhism that uses specialized tools like mantras and visualizations for rapid spiritual transformation.</term>, as soon as they see these "blood-drinking" deities, will recognize them as their own personal protectors <note>original: "tutelary deities"</note>. For such a practitioner, this meeting will feel like a reunion between human acquaintances. They will trust the deities; and by merging into them in complete unity, they will achieve **Perfect Awakening** <term>Buddhahood: The state of ultimate wisdom, compassion, and liberation from all suffering.</term>.

---

<note>These deities are associated with the brain's psychic center; they are the fierce or "wrathful" reflections of the Peaceful Deities seen in previous days.</note>

<note>The fear, terror, and awe felt by the deceased when seeing these deities only happen to those who have not had enough meditative training <note>original: "yogic training"</note> before death to recognize this state for what it is. For an advanced practitioner who can "take the Intermediate State by the forelock"—meaning they master death and know all appearances are unreal—there is no frightening "Bardo" to experience. Their goal is either an immediate conscious rebirth or, if they are fully spiritually mature, the state of **Ultimate Peace** <term>Nirvana: The final goal of the Buddhist path, signifying the end of suffering and rebirth.</term>.</note>

<note>As soon as one intense light fades, another begins. This leaves the deceased no room for distraction, keeping the mind concentrated and alert.</note>

<note>In this context, blood symbolizes existence within the cycle of suffering. "Drinking blood" represents the act of consuming and ultimately overcoming the thirst for worldly existence.</note>

## PART II: THE SECRET TEACHINGS

By having meditated on the description of these **blood-drinking deities** <note>original: "Herukas"; these are fierce, protective forms of enlightened beings that represent the transformation of negative emotions.</note> while in the human world, and by having performed some worship or praise of them; or, at least, by having seen their painted likenesses and their images, recognition of them will result upon witnessing the dawning of the deities at this stage, leading to liberation. In this lies the spiritual art.

Again, at the death of those **abbots** <term>Khenpos: Senior monastics who oversee the discipline and administration of a monastery.</term> and **scholars of philosophy** <term>Geshes: Highly educated monks who have spent decades studying Buddhist logic and metaphysics.</term> [who remain uninstructed in these **Intermediate State** <note>original: "Bardo"</note> teachings], no matter how diligently they may have devoted themselves to religious practices, and however clever they may have been in explaining doctrines while in the human world, there will not appear any miraculous signs such as a **rainbow-halo** <note>A phenomenon believed to appear in the sky during the funeral of a realized master.</note> [at the funeral pyre] nor **sacred relics** <term>Sharira: Small, pearl-like beads found among the ashes of a cremated saint, believed to be the physical essence of their spiritual realization.</term> [from the ashes]. 

This is because when they lived, the **secret teachings** <note>original: "esoteric doctrines"</note> were never held within their heart, and because they had spoken dismissively of them, and because they were never acquainted [through initiation] with the deities of the secret path. Thus, when these deities dawn in the **Intermediate State**, the scholars do not recognize them. Suddenly seeing what they had never seen before, they view the visions as hostile; and, because a feeling of antagonism is stirred within them, they pass into the miserable realms of rebirth. Therefore, if the followers of monastic discipline and the philosophers do not have the practices of the secret teachings within them, such signs as the rainbow-halo do not appear, nor are sacred relics or seed-like bones ever produced from their funeral pyres. These are the reasons for it.

For the practitioner who—even at this late stage—can be made to realize that these deities are merely the **karmic reflections** <note>original: "karmic personifications"</note> of their own mental habits, born from the experience of living, and who has the supreme power to face them without flinching, meeting them like old acquaintances and then merging their own identity into them, enlightenment regarding the true nature of **cyclical existence** <note>original: "sangsaric existence"</note> dawns. With this comes the **Perfect Illumination** called **Buddhahood** <term>Enlightenment: The state of being fully awake to the nature of reality, free from all suffering and delusion.</term>.

---

¹ The belief, common among many cultures since ancient times, that unusual phenomena usually mark the death and funeral of a great hero or saint, also exists among the Tibetans. The **Lamas** <term>Lamas: Spiritual teachers or masters in Tibetan Buddhism.</term> maintain that such phenomena have a rational spiritual explanation, as suggested in this text. Furthermore, they maintain that if a reputed saint be...

<summary>This page explains that recognition of the wrathful deities is possible through prior familiarity with their images and teachings. It warns that intellectual knowledge alone, without esoteric practice, leads to fear and unfavorable rebirth rather than spiritual signs like rainbows or relics.</summary>

# BARDO OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I]

The humblest of the humblest practitioners of the **Path of Secret Mantras** <note>original: "mantrayānic"; refers to the Vajrayana or Tantric path of Buddhism which uses ritual, mantra, and visualization.</note>—who may seem to be of very unrefined manners, lacking in industry and tact, and who may not live in strict accordance with his vows, and who in every way may be inelegant in his habits, and even unable, perhaps, to carry the practices of his teachings to a successful conclusion—let no one feel disrespect for nor doubt him, but rather pay reverence to the **secret teachings** <note>original: "esoteric [or mystic] doctrines"</note> which he holds. By that act of faith alone, one obtains liberation at this stage.

Even though the deeds of one paying such reverence may not have been very elegant while in the human world, at his death there will come at least one kind of miraculous sign, such as **rainbow-light** <note>A visual phenomenon often reported during the passing of a spiritual practitioner.</note>, **bone-images** <note>Spontaneous carvings of deities or symbols believed to appear on the bones of the deceased after cremation.</note>, and **relics** <term>Ring-sel: Small, pearl-like beads found in the ashes of a cremated master, viewed as physical evidence of spiritual realization.</term>. This is because the secret teachings possess great **spiritual blessings** <note>original: "gift-waves," a translation of the Tibetan word "jin-lap," which refers to a transformative power or grace transmitted from the lineage of teachers.</note>.¹

Those practitioners of the **Secret Mantra** <note>original: "mantrayānic devotees"</note> who are of average spiritual development or higher, who have meditated upon the **generation and completion stages** <note>original: "visualization and perfection processes"; the two primary phases of Tantric meditation where one first visualizes a deity and then dissolves that vision into the nature of the mind.</note> and practiced the **essential mantras** <note>original: "essence mantras"</note>,² need not wander down this far into the **Intermediate State of Reality** <note>original: "Chönyid Bardo"; the phase after death where the true nature of reality appears in the form of vivid lights and deities.</note>. As soon as they cease to breathe, they will be led into the pure paradise realms by the **Dakas and Dakinis** <note>original: "Heroes and Heroines"; celestial beings who assist practitioners on the path.</note> and the **Wisdom-Holders** <note>original: "Knowledge-Holders" or "Vidyadharas"; beings who have achieved high realization through the secret teachings.</note>.³ As a sign of [this success], when a practitioner is

---

<note>The following text continues from the main body, describing the physical signs of a saint's passing.</note>

really a saint, among the charred bones from his funeral pyre there will be found some shaped into beautiful forms like images, and that small pearl-like (or, as the text has it, seed-like) nodules will appear in the ashes of the bones cremated.

¹ That is to say, the secret teachings being realizable—because based on Truth itself—one who follows or even reverences them is, thereby, automatically brought into rapport with very definite spiritual forces.

² That is to say, those devotees who have practiced, in a thoroughly scientific manner, under a competent **teacher** <note>original: "guru"</note>, the intonation of certain sacred phrases called **essential mantras**. Examples of such phrases are: **"Hail to the Jewel in the Lotus!"** <note>original: "Oṃ Maṇi Padme Hūṃ"; the mantra of the Bodhisattva of Compassion.</note>; **"Hail to the Lord of Speech! Mūm"** <note>original: "Oṃ Wagi Shori Mūm"; the mantra of the Bodhisattva of Wisdom.</note>; **"Hail to the Wielder of the Scepter! Hūṃ"** <note>original: "Oṃ Vajra Pāṇi Hūṃ"; the mantra of the Bodhisattva of Power.</note>: these are the three essential mantras of "The Three Protectors" of Tibetan Buddhism; the first being the mantra of the **All-Seeing Lord** <note>Sanskrit: Avalokiteshvara; Tibetan: Chenrazee</note>, the "Great Pitier"; the second, that of the **Gentle Voice** <note>Sanskrit: Manjushri; Tibetan: Jampalyang</note>, the "God of Mystic Wisdom"; and the third, that of the **Vajra-in-Hand** <note>Sanskrit: Vajrapani; Tibetan: Chakdor</note>, the "Wielder of the Thunderbolt."

# PART II] PATHS OF LIBERATION

...at this time, the sky will be cloudless; they will merge into rainbow radiance; there will be sun-showers <note>Rain that falls while the sun is shining, often considered a highly auspicious sign in Tibetan culture.</note>, the sweet scent of incense in the air, music in the skies, and radiant lights. Sacred bone-relics and images will be found in their funeral pyre.

Therefore, for the abbots <note>Those who hold the monastic discipline.</note>, for the scholars, for those practitioners who have failed in their spiritual vows, and for all the common people, this *Great Liberation Through Hearing* <term>Thödol: The Tibetan title of the work, referring to liberation attained simply by hearing these instructions in the intermediate state.</term> is indispensable.¹ 

But those who have meditated upon the **Great Perfection** <term>Dzogchen: The "Great Perfection," a central teaching of the Nyingma school focused on realizing the mind's natural state.</term> and the **Great Symbol** <term>Mahamudra: The "Great Seal" or "Great Symbol," a meditative system emphasizing direct insight into the nature of mind, central to the Kagyu tradition.</term>² will recognize the **Clear Light** <term>Ösel: The fundamental, luminous nature of reality that dawns at the moment of death.</term> at the moment of death; and, by obtaining the **Truth Body** <term>Dharmakaya: The ultimate, formless dimension of enlightenment.</term>, none of them will need this *Thödol* to be read to them. 

By recognizing the Clear Light at the moment of death, they will also recognize the visions of the Peaceful and the Wrathful deities during the **Intermediate State of Reality** <term>Chönyid Bardo: The second stage of the afterlife where the deceased encounters the radiant projections of their own mind.</term> and obtain the **Enjoyment Body** <term>Sambhogakaya: The "Body of Bliss," a celestial form of a Buddha visible to highly realized practitioners.</term>. Or, by recognizing the truth during the **Intermediate State of Rebirth** <term>Sidpa Bardo: The final stage of the intermediate state where the soul is drawn toward a new life.</term>, they will obtain the **Emanation Body** <term>Nirmāṇa-Kāya: The physical form a Buddha takes to benefit sentient beings in the world.</term>; and, taking birth in the higher realms, they will meet with this Doctrine again in their next rebirth and enjoy the continuity of their spiritual path.³

> "May the fair, white, shining company of holy confessors be with you; may the victorious ranks of glorious martyrs surround you; may the joyful company of holy virgins receive you; and may the worthy fellowship of holy patriarchs open to you the place of rest and joy, and welcome you to be among them forever." <note>The original editor included this passage from Western liturgical traditions to show the universal human hope for a guided transition after death.</note>

¹ The teachers <note>original: "lāmas"</note> maintain that while goodness and book-learning are helpful, spiritual wisdom joined with unshakable faith is essential. A core precept taught to all new students is: "It is difficult to obtain liberation through intellectual knowledge alone; through faith, liberation is easily obtained."

² The "Great Perfection" refers to the teachings on attaining Buddhahood as taught by the school of **Padmasambhava** <note>The 8th-century Indian master who brought Buddhism to Tibet.</note>. The "Great Symbol" <note>Tibetan: *Chhag-chhen*; Sanskrit: *Mahā Mudrā*</note> refers to an ancient Indian system of yoga introduced to Tibet by the translator Marpa, who studied under the masters Atīsha and Naropa. **Milarepa**, the most beloved of Tibetan yogis and Marpa's successor, made the Great Symbol the foundation of his lineage.

³ If a person recognizes reality when it first appears—that is, if the dreamer in this worldly existence <note>original: "sang-sāric"</note> awakens into the divine state of the Enjoyment Body—the normal cycle of death and rebirth is broken. The awakened one then returns voluntarily and with full consciousness to the human world to help others.

<summary>This page explains that while the Great Liberation Through Hearing is essential for most people, highly realized masters who recognize the Clear Light of reality at death bypass the need for it. It describes the miraculous signs of a successful transition and the different "bodies" of a Buddha that a practitioner can attain.</summary>

<meta>This page transitions from the benefits of the "Liberation Through Hearing" for different classes of practitioners to the specific instructions for the Eighth Day of the intermediate state, marking the first appearance of the Fierce Manifestations.</meta>

# THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I

Therefore, this *Liberation Through Hearing* <note>original: "Thödol"</note> is the doctrine by which enlightenment <note>original: "Buddhahood"</note> may be attained without meditation; the doctrine liberating by the hearing [of it] alone; the doctrine which leads beings of great negative **past actions** <note>original: "karma"</note> on the Secret Path; the doctrine which produces differentiation instantaneously [between those who are initiated into it and those who are not]: being the profound doctrine which confers Perfect Enlightenment instantaneously. Those sentient beings who have been reached by it cannot go to the unhappy states of rebirth.

This [doctrine] and the *Liberation Through Wearing* <note>original: "Tahdol"</note> doctrine¹, when joined together being like unto a **sacred circle** <note>original: "maṇḍala"</note> of gold inset with turquoise, combine them.

Thus, the indispensable nature of the *Liberation Through Hearing* being shown, there now comes the setting-face-to-face with the dawning of the **Fierce Manifestations** <note>original: "Wrathful Deities"</note> in the **Intermediate State** <note>original: "Bardo"</note>.

### [THE EIGHTH DAY]

Again, calling the deceased by name, [address him] thus:

O nobly-born, listen undistractedly. Not having been able to recognize when the **Peaceful Manifestations** <note>original: "Peaceful Deities"</note> shone upon you in the **Intermediate State** above, you have come wandering thus far. Now, on the Eighth Day, the blood-drinking **Fierce Manifestations** will come to shine. Act so as to recognize them without being distracted.

---

...a Divine Incarnation, to work for the uplifting of mankind. If recognition be delayed until the **Intermediate State of Rebirth** <note>original: "Sidpa Bardo"</note>, and the **Emanation Body** <note>original: "Nirmāṇa-Kāya"; the physical form a Buddha takes to help worldly beings.</note> be attained, that is but a partial awakening, not an unclouded realization of Reality, the **Intermediate State of Rebirth** being a much lower plane than the **Intermediate State of Reality** <note>original: "Chönyid Bardo"</note>; but even then there will be won the great boon of spiritually enlightened birth on one of the higher planes—the **world of the gods** <note>original: "deva-loka"</note>, the **world of the titans** <note>original: "asura-loka"</note>, or the **human world** <note>original: "human-loka"</note>—and, upon being born anew in the human world, the devotee will take up, in virtue of acquired propensities gained in the previous earth-life, the study of the mystic **path of sacred sound** <note>original: "mantrayāna"</note> doctrines and **meditative union** <note>original: "yogic"</note> practices from the point where it was broken off by death—this being the continuity of **past actions** <note>original: "karma"</note>.

¹ Text: *Liberation Through Wearing* <note>original: "Btags-grol"; pronounced "Tah-dol"</note>, a small Tibetan work, consisting wholly of **sacred syllables** <note>original: "mantras"</note>, used as an accompaniment to the *Liberation Through Hearing*. If the deceased dies knowing the **sacred syllables of wearing**, they, being powerful talismans, give him safe passage through the **Intermediate State** and a happy rebirth. Very often a copy of the *Liberation Through Wearing* (or perhaps merely some of its **sacred syllables** copied on small strips of paper and wound together in a tiny roll) is tied to the corpse and burned or buried with it—just as a copy of the Egyptian *Book of the Dead* was ordinarily interred with a mummy.

## THE GREAT SACRED CIRCLE OF THE KNOWLEDGE-HOLDING AND WRATHFUL DEITIES

*Described on pages xxix-xxx, 127-8, 217-20*

# THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I]

Therefore, this *Liberation Through Hearing* <note>original: "Thödol"</note> is the teaching by which enlightenment <note>original: "Buddhahood"</note> may be attained without meditation; the teaching that liberates simply by hearing it; the teaching which leads beings with heavy negative **past actions** <note>original: "karma"</note> onto the Secret Path; the teaching which immediately distinguishes those who have been introduced to the truth from those who have not: being the profound teaching which grants Perfect Enlightenment in an instant. Those living beings who have encountered this teaching cannot fall into the lower realms of suffering rebirth.

Now, on the Eighth Day, the blood-drinking **Fierce Manifestations** will appear. Prepare yourself to recognize them without becoming distracted.

## PART II: THE ARISING OF THE HERUKA ORDER

O nobly-born, the <term>Great Glorious Buddha-Heruka</term> <note>original: "Dpal-chen-po Bud-dha Heruka"; the 'Great Glorious' wrathful manifestation of enlightenment</note>, dark-brown of color; with three heads, six hands, and four feet firmly postured; the right [face] being white, the left, red, the central, dark-brown; the body emitting flames of radiance; the nine eyes widely opened, in terrifying gaze; the eyebrows quivering like lightning; the protruding teeth glistening and set over one another; giving vent to sonorous utterances of 'a-la-la' and 'ha-ha', and piercing whistling sounds; the hair of a reddish-yellow color, standing on end, and emitting radiance; the heads adorned with dried [human] skulls, and the [symbols of the] sun and moon; black serpents and raw [human] heads forming a garland for the body; the first of the right hands holding a wheel, the middle one, a sword, the last one, a battle-axe; the first of the left hands, a bell, the middle one, a skull-bowl, the last one, a ploughshare; his body embraced by the Mother, <term>Buddha-Krotishaurima</term> <note>original: "Bud-dha Kro-ti-shva-ri-ma"; the Mighty Wrathful Mother, the female counterpart and consort to the Buddha-Heruka</note>, her right hand clinging to his neck and her left putting to his mouth a red shell [filled with blood], [making] a palatal sound like a crackling [and] a clashing sound, and a rumbling sound as loud as thunder; [emanating from the two deities] radiant flames of wisdom, blazing from every hair-pore [of the body] and each containing a flaming <term>ritual scepter</term> <note>original: "dorje"; a symbolic object representing the indestructible nature of reality, often called a diamond-bolt</note>; [the two deities together thus] standing with [one] leg bent and [the other] straight and tense, on a dais supported by <term>horned eagles</term> <note>original: "Garudas"; mythical bird-like beings who personify spiritual energy and aspiration</note>, will come forth from within your own brain and shine vividly upon you. Fear that not. Be not awed. Know it to be the embodiment of your own intellect. As it is your own tutelary deity, be not terrified. Be not afraid, for in reality it is <term>The Blessed One, the Illuminator</term> <note>original: "Bhagavān Vairochana"; usually seen as a peaceful white Buddha, here appearing in his transformative wrathful form</note>, the Father-Mother. PART II] DAWNING OF KARMIC ORDER

<term>Shaurima</term> <note>original: "shaurima"; one of the eight wrathful goddesses, often associated with the direction of the south-west</note>, her right hand clinging to his neck, the left offering to his mouth a red shell; the Father and Mother in union, issuing from the northern quarter of thy brain, will come to shine upon thee. Fear that not. Be not terrified. Be not awed. Recognize them to be the embodiment of thine own intellect. Since they are thine own <term>tutelary deity</term> <note>a personal meditation deity or spiritual protector</note>, be not afraid. In reality they are the <term>Father-Mother Blessed Lord of Infallible Success</term> <note>original: "Bhagavān Amogha-Siddhi"</note>. Believe; and be humble; and be fond of them. Concomitantly with recognition, liberation will come. Through such acknowledging, recognizing them to be tutelary deities, in <term>at-one-ment</term> <note>a state of mystical union or non-duality</note> thou wilt merge into them, and obtain <term>Buddhahood</term> <note>the state of complete spiritual awakening and enlightenment</note>.

Through the <term>spiritual teacher’s</term> <note>original: "guru's"</note> select teaching, one comes to recognize them to be the thought-forms issuing from one's own intellectual faculties. For instance, a person, upon recognizing a lion-skin to be a lion-skin, is freed from fear; for though it be only a stuffed lion-skin, if one do not know it to be so actually, fear arises, but, upon being told by some person that it is a lion-skin only, one is freed from fear. Similarly here, too, when the bands of <term>blood-drinking deities</term> <note>wrathful manifestations that represent the powerful, transformative energy of the enlightened mind</note>, huge of proportions, with very thick-set limbs, dawn as big as the skies, awe and terror are naturally produced in one. But as soon as the <term>setting-face-to-face</term> <note>the direct instruction or introduction to the nature of reality given by a teacher</note> is heard, one recognizes them to be one's own tutelary deities and one's own thought-forms. Then, when upon the <term>Mother Clear Light</term> <note>the fundamental, innate radiance of reality that exists eternally</note>—which one had been accustomed to formerly—a secondary Clear Light, the <term>Offspring Clear Light</term> <note>the clarity of awareness achieved through meditation during one's lifetime</note>, is produced, and the Mother and Offspring Clear Light, coming together like two intimate acquaintances, blend inseparably, and therefrom a self-emancipating radiance dawns upon one, through self-enlightenment and self-knowledge one is liberated.

### [THE THIRTEENTH DAY]

If this setting-face-to-face be not obtained, good persons on the Path <margin>Or "undergoing psychical development."</margin> too, fall back from here and wander into <term>the cycle of existence</term> <note>original: "Sangsāra"</note>. ### PART II] THE MIGHTY GODDESSES

The **Yoginīs of the South**, emerging from the southern quarter of your own brain, will come to shine upon you; do not be afraid of them.

O nobly-born, from the west there will dawn: the **Greenish-Black Vulture-Headed Eater-Goddess**, holding a baton in her hand; and the **Red Horse-Headed Delight-Goddess**, holding a large torso of a corpse; and the **White Eagle-Headed Mighty-Goddess**, holding a club in her hand; and the **Yellow Dog-Headed Female Demon** <note>original: "Rākshasī"; a type of fierce, often man-eating supernatural being</note>, holding a **ritual thunderbolt** <note>original: "dorje"; a symbolic weapon representing the indestructible nature of reality</note> in one hand and a ritual knife in the other, which she uses for cutting; and the **Red Hoopoe-Headed Desire-Goddess**, holding a bow and arrow aimed and ready; and the **Green Stag-Headed Wealth-Guardian Goddess**, holding an urn in her hand. O nobly-born, from the north there will dawn: the **Blue Wolf-Headed Wind-Goddess**, waving a pennant in her hand; and the **Red Ibex-Headed Woman-Goddess**, holding a pointed stake in her hand; and the **Black Sow-Headed Sow-Goddess**, holding a noose made of fangs in her hand; and the **Red Crow-Headed Thunderbolt-Goddess**, holding the corpse of an infant in her hand; and the **Greenish-Black Elephant-Headed Big-Nosed Goddess**,¹ holding a large corpse in her hand and drinking blood from a skull-cup; and the **Blue Serpent-Headed Water-Goddess**, holding a noose made of a serpent in her hand. These, the **Six Yoginīs of the North**, emerging from the northern quarter of your own brain, will come to shine upon you; do not be afraid of them.

O nobly-born, the **Four Yoginīs of the Door**, emerging from within your brain, will come to shine upon you: from the east, the **Black Cuckoo-Headed Mystic Goddess**,² holding an iron hook in her hand; from the south, the **Yellow Goat-Headed Mystic Goddess**, holding a noose in her hand; from the west, the **Red Lion-Headed Mystic Goddess**, holding an iron chain in her hand—

## PART II: RECOGNITION OF THOUGHT-FORMS

...equal to eighteen bodies such as your own body, set one upon another. Be not terrified at that; be not awed. If all existing phenomena shining forth as divine shapes and radiances are recognized to be the emanations of one's own intellect, **enlightenment** <note>original: "Buddhahood"</note> will be obtained at that very instant of recognition. The saying, "Enlightenment will be obtained in a moment" is that which applies now. Bearing this in mind, one will obtain enlightenment by merging, in a state of oneness, into the Radiances and the **Spiritual Bodies** <note>original: "Kāyas"</note>.

O nobly-born, whatever fearful and terrifying visions you may see, recognize them to be your own thought-forms.

O nobly-born, if you do not recognize them, and are frightened, then all the **Peaceful Deities** will shine forth in the shape of the **Great Black One** <note>original: "Mahā-Kāla"; a powerful protector deity representing the transition of time and the ultimate reality</note>;¹ and all the **Wrathful Deities** will shine forth in the form of the **King of Truth**, the **Lord of Death** <note>original: "Dharma-Rāja"; the judge of the dead who mirrors one's own conscience</note>;² and your own thought-forms becoming **Demonic Illusions** <note>original: "Māras"</note>, you will wander into the **Cycle of Rebirth** <note>original: "Sangsāra"</note>.

<insert>(O nobly-born, if one does not recognize one's own thought-forms, however learned one may be in the Scriptures—both the **General Teachings** and the **Esoteric Texts** <note>original: "Sūtras and Tantras"</note>—although practicing religion for an **aeon** <note>original: "kalpa"</note>, one does not obtain enlightenment. If one recognizes one's own thought-forms, by one important art and by one word, enlightenment is obtained.)</insert>

If one's thought-forms are not recognized as soon as one dies, the shapes of the **King of Truth**, the **Lord of Death**, will shine forth on the **Intermediate State of Reality** <note>original: "Chönyid Bardo"</note>. The largest of the bodies of the King of Truth, the Lord of Death, equaling the heavens in vastness; the intermediate, as large as **the Sacred Mountain at the Center of the World** <note>original: "Mt. Meru"</note>; the smallest, eighteen times one's own body, will come filling the world-systems. They will come having their upper teeth biting the lower lip; their eyes glassy; their hairs tied up on the top of the head; big-bellied, narrow-waisted; holding a **record of past deeds** <note>original: "karmic record"</note>—

---

¹ <note>The original Tibetan text "Mgon-po-Nag-po" (pronounced Gon-po-Nag-po) corresponds to the Sanskrit "Kāla-Nāth," commonly known in India as the Great Black One or Mahā-Kāla. At this stage, all the illusory forms of the Peaceful Deities blend and appear as this one deity.</note>
² <note>The original Tibetan "Gshin-rje-hi-chös-kyi-rgyal-po" (pronounced Shin-je-chö-kyi-gyal-po) combines the Sanskrit terms for "King of Truth" (Dharma-Rāja) and "King of the Dead" (Yama-Rāja). As described here and elsewhere in the Great Liberation through Hearing in the Bardo, this illusory deity commonly assumes many varied forms capable of merging into a single form.</note>

# 148 THE BARDO OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I]

->They hold a **record-board**¹ in their hands and shout "Strike! Slay!" They lick human brains, drink blood, tear heads from corpses, and rip out hearts. In this way, they will appear and fill the entire world.<-

O **nobly-born** <note>A traditional address to the deceased, reminding them of their spiritual heritage and potential for awakening</note>, when these thought-forms appear, do not be afraid or terrified. The body you now possess is a **mental-body** <term>Mental-body: A non-physical form composed of light and habitual tendencies that the deceased inhabits during the intermediate state</term> shaped by your past actions. Even if you are slain or chopped into pieces, you cannot die. Because your body is, in reality, a state of **emptiness** <note>original: "voidness"; the Buddhist concept of Sunyata, meaning that phenomena lack a solid, independent existence</note>, you have no need to fear.

The **Lords of Death** are also projections arising from the radiance of your own intellect. They are not made of solid matter; emptiness cannot be harmed by emptiness. Beyond the projections of your own mental faculties, these **Peaceful** and **Wrathful Deities**, the **Blood-Drinkers**, the many-headed beings, the rainbow lights, and the terrifying forms of the Lord of Death do not exist in objective reality. Of this, there is no doubt. By understanding this, all fear and terror will naturally vanish. By merging into a state of oneness with these visions, you will achieve **enlightenment** <note>original: "Buddhahood"</note>.

If you recognize the visions in this way, direct your faith and affection toward your **tutelary deities** <term>Tutelary deities: Personal protector gods or meditation deities (Yidam) that a practitioner relies upon for spiritual guidance</term>. Believe that they have come to guide you through the dangers of the **Bardo** <note>The intermediate state between death and rebirth</note>. Think, "I take refuge in them," and remember the **Precious Trinity** <note>The Three Jewels of Buddhism: the Buddha, the Dharma (Teachings), and the Sangha (Community)</note>, offering them your devotion. Whoever your personal protector deity may be, remember them now. Call them by name and pray:

***

<margin>¹ The Tibetan term is *khram-shing*, referring to a wooden board. This can mean a flogging-board used for punishment or, as it is used here, a board inscribed with the **karmic** <note>the spiritual record of one's intentional actions</note> records of the deceased's life. *Khram* refers to a scroll or inventory, and *shing* means "wood." We can translate this as "wood-register" or "record-board." In the great Tibetan epic, the *Kesar Saga* <note>original: *Ge-sar-bsgrungs*; a famous 8th or 9th-century oral epic about a heroic king</note>, it is said that the time and place of death are determined by the register of the Lords of Death. This concept bears a striking resemblance to the judgment scenes described in the ancient Egyptian *Book of the Dead*.</margin>

<summary>This page instructs the deceased to recognize the terrifying "Lords of Death" as mere projections of their own mind, emphasizing that the mental-body cannot be harmed by these empty forms. It encourages the deceased to maintain faith in their protector deities to achieve liberation from the cycle of rebirth.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the deceased in the intermediate state, moving from the recognition of terrifying visions to the specific prayers and invocations needed to navigate the Bardo. # PART II] PRAYERS FOR LIBERATION

> '[Alas!], I am wandering in the **intermediate state** <term>Bardo: The state of existence between death and rebirth</term>; run to my rescue;
> Uphold me by your grace, O Precious **Personal Protector** <term>Tutelary: A "Yidam" or meditation deity that serves as a personal guide through spiritual transitions</term>!'

Calling upon the name of your own **spiritual teacher** <note>original: "guru"</note>, pray in this way:

> '[Alas!] I am wandering in the **Bardo**; rescue me!
> [O] let not your grace forsake me!'

Have faith in the **Blood-Drinking Deities** <note>These are the wrathful manifestations of the Buddhas, representing the powerful, transformative energy of the enlightened mind</note>, as well, and offer up this prayer:

> 'Alas! when [I am] wandering in the **cycle of suffering** <note>original: "Sangsāra"</note>, through the force of overpowering illusions,
> On the light-path of the abandonment of fright, fear, and awe,
> May the bands of the **Blessed Ones** <note>original: "Bhagavāns"</note>, the Peaceful and Wrathful Ones, lead [me];
> May the bands of the Wrathful Goddesses Rich in Space be [my] rear-guard,
> And save me from the fearful ambushes of the **Bardo**,
> And place me in the state of the Perfectly-Enlightened Buddhas.
> When wandering alone, separated from dear friends,
> When the empty forms of one's own thoughts are shining here,
> May the Buddhas, exerting the force of their grace,
> Cause not to come the fear, awe, and terror in the **Bardo**.
> When the five bright Wisdom-Lights are shining here,
> May recognition come without dread and without awe;
> When the divine bodies of the Peaceful and the Wrathful are shining here,
> May the assurance of fearlessness be obtained and the **Bardo** be recognized.
> When, by the power of negative **actions** <term>Karma: The law of cause and effect where past deeds influence future experiences</term>, misery is being tasted,
> May the tutelary deities dissipate the misery;
> When the natural sound of Reality is reverberating [like] a thousand thunders,
> May they be transformed into the sounds of the **Six Syllables**.¹

---

¹ These are the essence **sacred chant** <note>original: "mantra"</note> of **the Lord of Compassion** <note>original: "Chenrazee" or "Avalokiteshvara"</note>, being *Om-Ma-ni-Pad-me-Hum*. As the patron deity of Tibet, his mantra is credited with bringing to an end the cycle of rebirth and providing entrance into a higher state of being.

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the deceased in the intermediate state, moving from the recognition of terrifying visions to the specific prayers and invocations needed to navigate the Bardo. ### 150 BARDO OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS [BOOK I

> When unprotected, **actions and their consequences** <note>original: "karma"</note> having to be followed here,
> I beseech the Gracious Compassionate One¹ to protect me;
> When suffering miseries of **habitual tendencies of action** <note>original: "karmic propensities"</note> here,
> May the blissfulness of the **Inner Light** <note>original: "Clear Light"</note> dawn;
> May the **Five Elements**² not rise up as enemies;
> But may I behold the realms of the Five Orders of the
> Enlightened Ones.'

Thus, in earnest faith and humility, offer up the prayer; whereby all fears will vanish and Buddhahood in the **Body of Perfect Enjoyment** <note>original: "Saṃbhoga-Kāya," a divine form of a Buddha visible to those with high spiritual realization</note> will undoubtedly be won: important is this. Being undistracted, repeat it in that manner, three or [even] seven times.

into **liberation from suffering** <note>original: "Nirvāṇa"</note>; hence its importance in the **intermediate state** <note>original: "Bardo"</note> prayer. In the Tibetan work called *The Hundred Thousand Words on the Jewel Mantra* <note>original: "Mani-bkah-hbum"</note>, that is, the 'History of the **Jewel Mantra** <note>original: "Māṇi" or sacred formula of the Lord of Compassion</note>' this **sacred formula** <note>original: "mantra"</note> is said to be 'the essence of all happiness, prosperity, and knowledge, and the great means of liberation'; also it is said that the syllable **Om** <note>original: "ōm"</note> closes the door of rebirth among the gods, **ma** <note>original: "mā"</note>, among the **titans** <note>original: "asuras"</note>, **ni** <note>original: "ṇi"</note>, among mankind, **pe** <note>original: "pāy"</note>, among sub-human creatures, **me** <note>original: "mē"</note>, among **unhappy ghosts** <note>original: "pretas"</note>, and **hung** <note>original: "hūng"</note>, among the inhabitants of Hell. Accordingly, each of the six syllables is given the colour of the light-path corresponding to the six states of existence, thus: **Om**, the white light-path of the **world of the gods** <note>original: "deva-loka"</note>; **ma**, the green light-path of the **world of the titans** <note>original: "asura-loka"</note>; **ni**, the yellow light-path of the **human world** <note>original: "manaka-loka"</note>; **pe**, the blue light-path of the **animal world** <note>original: "tiryaka-loka"</note>; **me**, the red light-path of the **ghost world** <note>original: "preta-loka"</note>; and **hung**, the smoke-coloured or black light-path of the **hell world** <note>original: "naraka-loka"</note>.

There is an old Tibetan folk-tale concerning a religious devotee who tried to incline his irreligious mother to devotional observances and merely succeeded in habituating her to the recitation of this **sacred formula**. Her bad **actions** <note>original: "karma"</note> predominating over her good **actions**, at death she passed into the Hell-world, whereupon her son, being proficient in **meditative discipline** <note>original: "yoga"</note>, went to her rescue; and she, upon seeing him, was able, in virtue of having recited the **sacred formula** on earth, to recite it in Hell, and instantaneously she and all who heard it were liberated from Hell: for, as the tale at its end teaches, 'Such is the power of the **sacred formula**'.

The origin of this **sacred formula** is traceable through **treasure-revealer** <note>original: "tertön"</note> works concerning the introduction (during the eighth century) of Tantric Buddhism into Tibet. Dr. Waddell is inclined to doubt that these **treasure-revealer** works were hidden away then (that is, in the time of the **Lotus-Born Master** <note>original: "Padma Sambhava"</note>) and in later centuries recovered, as the **treasure-revealers** (that is, 'takers-out' of such lost books) claim, and suggests that their compilation dates from the fourteenth to the sixteenth century—a tentative and possibly unsound theory (see L. A. Waddell, *Lamaism in Sikhim*, in the *Gazetteer of Sikhim*, edited by H. H. Risley, Calcutta, 1894, page 289; also our Introduction, pages 73-7). In any case, the **sacred formula**, at least by tradition (which ordinarily is as reliable as recorded history), seems to have come into, or been originated in, Tibet contemporaneously with the introduction of Buddhism into Tibet.

¹ That is, **The Lord of Compassion** <note>original: "Chenrazee" or Avalokiteshvara</note>.
² These are: Earth, Air, Water, Fire, and Ether.

<summary>This page presents a powerful prayer to navigate the Bardo, followed by a detailed explanation of the six-syllable mantra 'Om Mani Padme Hum.' It explains how each syllable corresponds to closing the doors of rebirth in the six realms of existence, illustrating the mantra's power through a folk tale and a discussion of its historical origins in Tibet.</summary>

# PART II] ATTAINING FREEDOM FROM REBIRTH <note>original: "WINNING OF LIBERATION"</note>

However heavy the burden of **actions and their consequences** <note>original: "karma"</note> may be, and however weak the remaining positive merit may be, it is not possible that **freedom from the cycle of suffering** <note>original: "liberation"</note> will not be obtained [if one but recognize the truth]. If, nevertheless, despite everything done in these [stages of the **intermediate state** <note>original: "Bardo"</note>], recognition is still not brought about, then—there being danger of one's wandering further, into the third **intermediate state**, called the **Intermediate State of Rebirth** <note>original: "Sidpa Bardo"</note>—the instructions for facing that stage will be shown in detail hereafter.

### [THE CONCLUSION, SHOWING THE FUNDAMENTAL IMPORTANCE OF THE TEACHINGS FOR THE INTERMEDIATE STATE]

Whatever the religious practices of any person may have been—whether extensive or limited—various misleading illusions occur during the moments of death; and hence this **Great Liberation through Hearing** <note>original: "Thödol"</note> is indispensable. To those who have meditated much, the real Truth dawns as soon as the body and the **life-force** <note>original: "consciousness-principle"</note> part. The acquiring of experience while living is important: they who have [then] recognized [the true nature of] their own being,¹ and thus have had some experience, obtain great power during the **Intermediate State of the Moments of Death**, when the **Inner Radiance** <note>original: "Clear Light"</note> dawns.

(Again, the meditation on the deities of the Mystic Path of the **Sacred Formula** <note>original: "Mantra"</note>, [both in the] visualizing and the perfecting stages while living, will be of great influence when the peaceful and wrathful visions dawn in the **Intermediate State of Reality** <note>original: "Chönyid Bardo"</note>.) Thus, the training in this **intermediate state** <note>original: "Bardo"</note> being of particular importance even while living,² hold to it, read it, commit it to memory, bear it in mind properly, and read it regularly three times; let the words and the meanings be very clear; it should be so deeply learned that the words and the meanings will not be forgotten even though a hundred executioners were pursuing [you].

---

¹ Literally, 'intellect' or 'the principle of consciousness'.
² Compare the following passage from the medieval text *The Book of the Craft of Dying* <note>original: "Ars Moriendi"</note>, chapter V (p. 37): 'Whatever man desires, and wishes to die well and surely and with merit, without danger, he must take heed, and study and learn diligently this craft of dying, and the preparations for it described above, while he is in health; and not wait until death enters into him.'

<summary>This page concludes the instructions for the first two stages of the Bardo, emphasizing that spiritual preparation during life is the most effective way to recognize the Clear Light and avoid wandering into the next stage of rebirth.</summary>
<keywords>Karma, Bardo, Sidpa Bardo, Thödol, Mantra, Chönyid Bardo, Clear Light, liberation, craft of dying, consciousness, meditation</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the section on the Intermediate State of Reality (Chönyid Bardo), emphasizing the efficacy of the "Great Liberation by Hearing" for all beings, regardless of their past actions. It serves as a final exhortation on the importance of the text itself before transitioning further into the funerary rites.</meta>

->BARDO OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS<-

It is called the **Great Liberation by Hearing**, because even those who have committed the <term>five boundless sins</term> <note>original: "five boundless sins"; these are: killing one's father, killing one's mother, causing schism in the religious community, killing a saint (Arhat), and intentionally drawing the blood of a Buddha.</note> are sure to be liberated if they hear it with their own ears. Therefore, read it in the midst of large gatherings. Spread it widely. By hearing it just once, even if one does not fully understand it, the words will be remembered in the <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: "Bardo"</note> without a single word being forgotten, for the intellect becomes nine times more clear and lucid there. For this reason, it should be proclaimed in the ears of all living people; it should be read at the pillows of all who are sick; it should be read at the side of all corpses: it should be spread far and wide.

Those who encounter this <insert>doctrine</insert> are truly fortunate. Except for those who have accumulated great spiritual merit and cleared away many of their mental obscurations, it is difficult to even meet with it. Even when one does encounter it, it is difficult to grasp. Yet, liberation will be won simply by not disbelieving it upon hearing it. Therefore, treat this teaching with great care: it is the essence of all spiritual paths. <note>The woodblock-print version of the text adds: "This is the **Tantra** (esoteric system) of all doctrines."</note>

The **Setting-Face-to-Face** <note>A ritual introduction to the true nature of reality.</note> while experiencing Reality in the **Intermediate State**, called ‘The Teaching Which Liberates By Merely Being Heard And That Which Liberates By Merely Being Attached,’ <note>This refers to the *Thadol* or "Tibetan Book of the Dead."</note> is finished. <note>The block-print text, which matches our manuscript in almost every important detail, concludes this section for the **Intermediate State of Reality** (original: *Chönyid Bardo*) with a slightly different sentence: "The Teaching for the Intermediate State, the Setting-Face-to-Face while experiencing Reality, from *The Great Liberation by Hearing While in the Intermediate State, Liberating by Merely Being Heard, and Liberating By Merely Being Seen*, is finished."</note>

<summary>This passage emphasizes the transformative power of the "Great Liberation by Hearing," asserting that simply hearing the text can lead to spiritual freedom in the afterlife. It instructs practitioners to share the teaching with the living, the dying, and the dead as the ultimate spiritual essential.</summary>

->THE INTERMEDIATE STATE OF KARMIC ILLUSIONS<-

It is called the **Great Liberation by Hearing**, because even those who have committed the <term>five boundless sins</term> <note>original: "five boundless sins"; these are five extremely heavy negative actions: killing one's father, killing one's mother, killing an enlightened being (Arhat), intentionally drawing the blood of a Buddha, or causing a split in the community of practitioners.</note> are sure to reach liberation if they hear these words. This is because the mind becomes nine times more clear and lucid in that state. For this reason, these teachings should be proclaimed in the ears of the living; they should be read at the pillows of the sick; they should be read beside the bodies of the dead. It must be spread far and wide.

Even when one does encounter it, its meaning is difficult to grasp. Yet, liberation will be achieved simply by not doubting it upon hearing it. 'The essence of all things is one and the same, perfectly calm and tranquil, and shows no sign of "becoming" <note>In Buddhist philosophy, "becoming" refers to the constant cycle of birth, change, and death; the absolute essence is seen as beyond this change.</note>; ignorance, however, is in its blindness and delusion oblivious of **Enlightenment** <note>original: "Bodhi"; the state of complete spiritual awakening and the realization of ultimate truth.</note>, and, on that account, cannot recognize truthfully all those conditions, differences, and activities which characterize the phenomena of the Universe.'

—**Ashvaghosha** <note>An influential 2nd-century Indian philosopher and poet whose work helped define the Mahayana tradition.</note>. ***The Awakening of Faith*** <note>A foundational text of Mahayana Buddhism that explains how the pure mind becomes obscured by worldly thoughts and how to return to the source.</note> (**Daisetz Teitaro Suzuki's** Translation <note>Suzuki was a prominent Japanese scholar whose early 20th-century translations were instrumental in introducing Eastern philosophy to the West.</note>).

<column-break/>

<term>Mahayana Buddhism</term> <note>The "Great Vehicle" of Buddhism, which emphasizes compassion and the attainment of Buddhahood for the sake of all sentient beings.</note>

<term>Philosophy</term> <note>The intellectual investigation of the nature of reality, existence, and the mind.</note>

<summary>This page features an epigraph by the sage Ashvaghosha explaining that while reality is inherently tranquil and unified, human ignorance creates the perception of a fragmented and changing universe. ## [PART I]

### [THE WORLD BEYOND DEATH]

[Introductory Instructions to the Priest]: Although many clear reminders have been given during the previous stage (the Intermediate State of Reality), recognition of the truth remains difficult for certain people. Aside from those who are already very familiar with the ultimate Truth and those who have positive <term>spiritual merit</term> <note>original: *karma*; the principle that one's past actions shape their future reality.</note>, many will struggle. This includes those with negative merit who lacked spiritual practice, or those whose fear and terror at the visions of death prevent them from understanding what is happening. These spirits continue their journey down to the Fourteenth Day; to help them recognize their situation clearly, the following sections must be read.

### [THE SPIRIT BODY: ITS BIRTH AND SUPERNATURAL POWERS]

Worship having been offered to the <term>Three Jewels</term> <note>original: "the Trinity"; the Buddha, the teachings, and the spiritual community.</note>, and the prayer calling for the help of the <term>Enlightened Beings</term> <note>original: Buddhas and *Bodhisattvas*; those who have achieved or are nearing enlightenment and help others do the same.</note> having been recited, then, calling the deceased person by name three or seven times, speak these words:

"O nobly-born one, listen well, and take to heart that this birth..."

...in the <term>Hell-world</term>, in the <term>Realm of the Gods</term> <note>original: *Deva-world*</note>, and in this <term>Intermediate State body</term> <note>original: *Bardo-body*</note> is of the kind called <term>supernormal birth</term>. <note>original: *rdzüs-skyes* (pronounced zü-kyé). This refers to being born "by transformation" or in a "disguised" manner. Unlike human birth which requires parents and a womb, this birth is an instantaneous, supernormal appearance of the consciousness in a new form.</note>

Indeed, when you were experiencing the radiances of the Peaceful and the Wrathful deities in the <term>Intermediate State of Reality</term> <note>original: *Chönyid Bardo*; the stage of death where the basic nature of reality appears as bright lights and deities.</note>, you were unable to recognize them and fainted away from fear. This lasted for about four days <note>The text likely contains a transcription error here; traditional teachings usually specify three and one-half days of unconsciousness following death.</note> after your passing. Then, when you recovered from that swoon, your <term>Consciousness</term> <note>original: *Knower*</note> must have risen up in its original condition, and a radiant body, resembling your former body, must have appeared—just as the <term>Esoteric Scriptures</term> <note>original: *Tantra*</note> say:

> ‘Having a body [seemingly] fleshly [resembling] the former
> and the one yet to be produced,
> Endowed with all sense-faculties and the power of unimpeded
> motion,
> Possessing <term>miraculous powers of past actions</term> <note>original: *karmic miraculous powers*; the ability to move through walls or travel instantly, driven by the force of one's past deeds rather than physical effort.</note>,
> Visible to the pure celestial eyes [of other Intermediate beings] of a similar
> nature.’

Such, then, is the teaching.

That radiant body—described as resembling "the former and that to be produced" (meaning you will have a body just like the human body of flesh and blood you used to have, shaped by your mental habits)—will also be endowed with certain signs and beauties of perfection, such as beings destined for a high rebirth possess. <note>This appearance of the "Bardo-body" happens instantly, described by Tibetan teachers as being "like a trout leaping forth from water."</note>

This body, born of desire, is a mental hallucination in the <term>Intermediate State</term>, and it is called the <term>desire-body</term>.

At that time—if you are to be born as a <term>god</term> <note>original: *deva*</note>—visions of the <term>God Realm</term> will appear to you; similarly, wherever you are destined to be born—whether as a <term>jealous god</term> <note>original: *asura*</note>, or a human being, or...

[Instructions to the Officiant]: Thus speak, and, if recognition result from that, Liberation will be obtained, without need of the wandering in the <term>Six Realms of Existence</term> <note>original: *Six Lokas*; the various worlds of rebirth including those of gods, humans, animals, and others.</note>. If, however, through influence of <term>past actions</term> <note>original: *karma*</note>, recognition is made difficult, thereupon say as follows:

O nobly-born, again listen. 'Endowed with all sense-faculties and power of unimpeded motion' implieth [that although] thou mayst have been, when living, blind of the eye, or deaf, or lame, yet on this After-Death Plane thine eyes will see forms, and thine ears will hear sounds, and all other sense-organs of thine will be unimpaired and very keen and complete. Wherefore the <term>Intermediate State body</term> <note>original: *Bardo*-body</note> hath been spoken of as 'endowed with all sense-faculties'. That [condition of existence, in which thou thyself now art] is an indication that thou art deceased and wandering in the <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: *Bardo*</note>. Act so as to know this. Remember the teachings; remember the teachings.

O nobly-born, 'unimpeded motion' implieth that thy present body being a <term>desire-body</term> <note>A subtle body formed by the mind's habits and desires, rather than physical matter.</note>—thine intellect having been separated from its seat<sup>2</sup>—is not a body of gross matter, so that now thou hast the power to go right through any rock-masses, hills, boulders, earth, houses, and <term>Mount Meru</term> <note>The sacred mountain at the center of the universe in Buddhist and Hindu cosmology.</note> itself without being impeded.<sup>3</sup> Excepting <term>Bodh Gaya</term> <note>original: *Budh-Gayā*; the holy site in India where the Buddha attained enlightenment.</note> and the mother's womb,<sup>4</sup> even

---

<sup>1</sup> Or 'directly above'; or literally, 'as being upon the crown of thy head'. The significance herein is <term>hidden</term> <note>original: *occult*</note>: the <term>Crown Opening</term> <note>original: *Brāhmanic Aperture*; the point at the top of the head where consciousness exits the body.</note>, through which the consciousness-principle normally departs from the human body, either temporarily in <term>meditative trance</term> <note>original: *yogic trance*</note> or permanently at death, is upon the crown of the head; and if the visualization be centered directly over that aperture very definite psychic or spiritual benefit accrues to the visualizer. (Compare page 92, note 1.)

<sup>2</sup> The 'seat' is the human body which has been left behind.

<sup>3</sup> This power, supernormal in the human world, is normal in the <term>four-dimensional</term> after-death state. In the human world, such powers, innate in all persons, can be developed and exercised through proficiency in <term>spiritual discipline</term> <note>original: *yoga*</note>. The Buddha describes some of them thus: 'In this case suppose that a being enjoyeth the possession, in various ways, of mystic power: from being one, he becometh multiform; from being multiform, he becometh one; from being visible, he becometh invisible; he passeth without hindrance to the further side of a wall or battlement, or a mountain, as if through air; he walketh on water without dividing it, as if on solid ground; he travelleth cross-legged through the sky, like the birds on the wing'—(<term>The Chapter of the Brahmins</term> <note>original: *Brāhmaya Vagga*</note> in <term>The Collection of Numerical Discourses</term> <note>original: *Anguttara Nikāya*</note>).

<sup>4</sup> Unless previously endowed with a very high degree of spiritual enlighten-

<header>PART I] MIRACULOUS POWERS</header>

ment, the deceased cannot consciously go to these two places at will; for from <term>Bodh Gaya</term> <note>original: Budh-Gayā; the site in India where the Buddha attained enlightenment, considered a powerful spiritual center.</note> and from the mother’s womb (as being the destined path to rebirth) radiate such spiritually blinding lights that the ordinary mind would be overcome with fear, just as it is when various lights appear in the <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: Bardo</note>, and would therefore avoid them. (Compare with stanza 6, ‘The Path of Good Wishes Which Protecteth from Fear in the Bardo’, p. 206.)

The King of Mountains, <term>Mount Meru</term> <note>The central mountain of the universe in traditional Buddhist cosmology.</note> itself, can be passed through by you, straight forwards and backwards without any hindrance. That, too, is a sign that you are wandering in the <term>Intermediate State of Seeking Rebirth</term> <note>original: *Sidpa Bardo*</note>. Remember your <term>spiritual teacher’s</term> <note>original: *guru’s*</note> teachings, and pray to the Compassionate Lord.

O nobly-born, you are actually endowed with the power of miraculous action,<sup>1</sup> which is not, however, the result of any <term>meditative concentration</term> <note>original: *samādhi*</note>, but a power that has come to you naturally; and, therefore, it is of the nature of <term>karmic power</term> <note>original: *karmic power*; power resulting from the natural laws of cause and effect after death rather than spiritual practice.</note>.<sup>2</sup> You are able in a moment to cross the <term>four continents</term> surrounding Mount Meru.<sup>3</sup> Or you can instantaneously arrive in whatever place you wish; you have the power of reaching there within the time it takes a man to bend or stretch out his hand. Do not desire these various powers of illusion and shape-shifting; do not desire them.<sup>4</sup>

There is none [of such powers] which you might desire that you cannot perform. The ability to exercise them without hindrance exists in you now. Know this, and pray to the <term>teacher</term> <note>original: *guru*</note>.

O nobly-born, ‘Visible to pure celestial eyes of like nature’

---

<sup>1</sup> <note>original Tibetan: *rdzu-hphrul* (pronounced zu-ül)</note> *Miraculous power*: *rdzu* meaning ‘power to change one’s shape’; and *hphrul* meaning ‘power to change one’s size and number’, by appearing or disappearing at will, as one or as many, large or small. If developed on the earth-plane through <term>spiritual disciplines</term> <note>original: *yogic* practices</note>, such miraculous power becomes a permanent gift, and can be used in the body or out of the body (as when in the <term>Intermediate State</term>).

<sup>2</sup> The text implies that the deceased possesses this miraculous power as a result of being—through the workings of <term>cause and effect</term> <note>original: *karma*</note>—in the Intermediate State, where such power is natural, and not because of merit earned through the practice of <term>disciplined meditation</term> <note>original: *yoga*</note> while in the human body.

<sup>3</sup> See pages 62–65, concerning the structure of the universe.

<sup>4</sup> The most advanced <term>masters</term> <note>original: *lāmas*</note> teach the student not to strive after psychic powers of this nature for their own sake; for until the student is morally fit to use them wisely, they become a serious obstacle to higher spiritual growth. Not until the lower or passionate nature of man is completely mastered is he safe in using them.

<page-num>160</page-num>
# BARDO OF SEEKING REBIRTH [BOOK II]

This means that those beings of a similar nature—those who are at a similar level of spiritual understanding or constitution within the <term>Intermediate State</term> <note>original: Bardo; the state between death and rebirth.</note>—will be able to see one another. For example, those beings who are destined to be born among the <term>celestial beings</term> <note>original: *devas*; gods or light-beings in Buddhist cosmology.</note> will see each other, and so on. Do not become fixated on those you see, but instead focus your mind in meditation upon the <term>Compassionate One</term> <note>original: The Compassionate One refers to Chenrezig (Avalokiteshvara), the patron deity of Tibet who embodies the compassion of all Buddhas.</note>.

The phrase "Visible to pure celestial eyes" also means that the <term>celestial beings</term> <note>original: *devas*</note>, who are born in a state of purity due to their past good deeds, are visible to the "celestial eyes" of those who practice deep <term>meditative concentration</term> <note>original: *dhyāna*</note>. However, these practitioners will not see them at all times; when their minds are perfectly concentrated, they see them, but when they are not, they do not. Sometimes, even while practicing <term>meditation</term>, they may become distracted and lose the vision.

### [CHARACTERISTICS OF EXISTENCE IN THE INTERMEDIATE STATE]

O <term>nobly-born</term> <note>A respectful term used to address the deceased person, reminding them of their spiritual potential.</note>, as the possessor of this kind of subtle body, you will see your familiar places on earth and your relatives as if you were seeing them in a dream.

You will see your relatives and friends and try to speak to them, but you will receive no reply. Then, seeing them and your family weeping for you, you will think, "I am dead! What shall I do?" This realization will cause you great misery, feeling just like a fish cast out of the water onto hot sand.

---

1. In addition to normal human eyes with their limited vision, the <term>spiritual teachers</term> <note>original: *lāmas*</note> say there are five types of eyes: (1) Eyes of Instinct (or Eyes of the Flesh), like those of predatory birds and beasts, which often see further than human eyes; (2) <term>Celestial Eyes</term>, like the eyes of the <term>gods</term> <note>original: *devas*</note>, which can see the human world, their own world, and the past and future lives of beings in both; (3) Eyes of Truth, like the eyes of <term>enlightened beings</term> <note>original: *Bodhisattvas* and *Arhants*</note>, capable of seeing through hundreds of <term>world-periods</term> <note>original: *kalpas*; vast cycles of time in Buddhist cosmology.</note> into the past and future; (4) Divine Eyes, belonging to the most advanced <term>enlightened beings</term> <note>original: *Bodhisattvas*</note>, who can see through millions of world-periods; and (5) Eyes of Wisdom belonging to the <term>Buddhas</term>, which can see through all of eternity.

2. Usually, <term>celestial beings</term> are seen only when a person has developed "clear-seeing" (clairvoyance) through <term>meditation</term> <note>original: *dhyāna*</note>, though they occasionally appear unexpectedly. The <term>Buddhist Scriptures</term> <note>original: *Tri-Pitaka*; meaning "The Three Baskets" of the teachings.</note>, much like the sacred writings of other world religions, are full of accounts of visions and visitations from these higher beings.

<vocab>Intermediate State, celestial beings, meditation, celestial eyes, enlightened beings, perfected ones, world-periods, Buddhas, The Three Baskets of Scripture, spiritual teachers</vocab>

<summary>This page describes how the deceased perceives other spirits and their living relatives in the Intermediate State, emphasizing the clarity of spiritual vision and the emotional pain of realizing one's own death. It explains the hierarchy of "five eyes" or levels of spiritual perception available to different beings.</summary>

<keywords>Intermediate State, Bardo, celestial beings, dhyāna, five eyes, grief, spiritual vision, karma, Chenrezig</keywords>

The text emphasizes the importance of focus and prayer to avoid being overwhelmed by the confusion of this state.</meta>

# PART I] THE WIND OF <term>KARMA</term> <note>original: karma; the law of cause and effect, where one's past actions and intentions determine future experiences.</note>

[of water] on red-hot embers. Such misery you will be experiencing at present. But feeling miserable will be of no use to you now. If you have a divine **<term>spiritual teacher</term>** <note>original: guru; a personal religious guide.</note>,¹ pray to him. Pray to your personal Protector Deity, the **Compassionate One** <note>This refers to Chenrezig (Avalokiteshvara), the embodiment of universal compassion.</note>. Even though you feel attachment for your relatives and connections, it will do you no good. So do not be attached. Pray to the Compassionate Lord; you shall have nothing of sorrow, or of terror, or of awe.

O **nobly-born** <note>A respectful address reminding the deceased of their spiritual heritage and potential for enlightenment.</note>, when you are driven [hither and thither] by the ever-moving wind of **<term>karma</term>**, your intellect, having no object upon which to rest, will be like a feather tossed about by the wind, riding on the "horse of breath."² Ceaselessly and against your will, you will be wandering about. To all those who are weeping [you will say], "Here I am; do not weep." But because they cannot hear you, you will think, "I am dead!" And again, at that time, you will feel very miserable. Do not be miserable in that way.

There will be a grey twilight-like light, both by night and by day, and at all times.³ In that kind of **Intermediate State** <note>original: Bardo; the transitional realm between death and the next life.</note> you will remain for either one, two, three, four, five, six, or seven weeks, until the forty-ninth day.⁴ It has been said that ordinarily the miseries of the **<term>Intermediate State of Seeking Rebirth</term>** <note>original: Sidpa Bardo; the final stage of the afterlife where the soul is drawn toward a new womb.</note> are experienced for about twenty-two days; but, because of the determining influence of **<term>karma</term>**, a fixed period is not certain.

O **nobly-born**, at about that time, the fierce wind of **<term>karma</term>**, terrifying and hard to endure, will drive you [onwards], from behind, in dreadful gusts. Do not fear it. That is your own...

---

¹ This refers to a superhuman **<term>spiritual teacher</term>** <note>original: guru</note> of the **Divine Stream** Order <note>original: Divyaugha Order; a lineage of celestial or highly realized masters.</note>. (See Addenda, p. 222.)
² Like the restless wind, **<term>karma</term>** is always in motion, and the intellect—when it lacks the support of a physical human body—is easily blown about by it.
³ **<term>Practitioners of yoga</term>** <note>original: Yogis</note> explain this by saying that in the **Intermediate State** body—which is a "mind-born" body of desire—the nervous system of the physical world is missing, so the light of the sun, moon, and stars is not visible. Only the natural light of nature (referred to by medieval alchemists and mystics as the "astral light") can be seen; and this "astral light" is said to be spread everywhere through the air like a constant earthly twilight, yet it is bright enough for the eyes of the ethereal beings in the **Intermediate State**. (Cf. p. 92¹.)
⁴ See our Introduction, pp. 6-7.

# [PART I: THE BARDO OF SEEKING REBIRTH]

...illusion. A thick, terrifying darkness will appear before you continually, and from the midst of it, you will hear frightening shouts like "Strike! Kill!" and similar threats. <note>The deceased, driven by the karmic effects of selfishness during their human life, may hallucinate that all other beings in this state are enemies. This is essentially a waking nightmare born of past habits.</note> Do not be afraid of these.

In other cases, for those who have accumulated a great deal of negative **<term>karma</term>** <note>original: karma; the law of cause and effect where past actions shape future experiences.</note>, flesh-eating **<term>demons</term>** <note>original: rākshasas; fearsome, predatory spirits in Buddhist mythology.</note> carrying various weapons will appear, shouting "Strike! Kill!" and making a terrifying noise. They will seem to rush at you, competing to see which of them can catch you first. You will also experience frightening illusions of being hunted by savage beasts of prey. You may see snow, heavy rain, or total darkness, and feel fierce gusts of wind. You might hallucinate that you are being chased by many people, or hear the overwhelming sounds of mountains collapsing, stormy seas crashing, fire roaring, and fierce winds howling. <note>These sounds represent the "vital forces" of the four elements (earth, water, fire, and air) as the physical body's connection to the consciousness disintegrates.</note>

When you hear these sounds, you will be terrified and will try to flee in any direction, not caring where you go. But your path will be blocked by three terrifying, deep precipices—one white, one black, and one red. You will feel as if you are about to fall down into them. 

O **nobly-born** <note>A reminder of the deceased's spiritual potential.</note>, these are not actually physical cliffs; they are the mental projections of **Anger, Lust, and Stupidity**. <note>original: Anger, Lust, and Stupidity; also known as the "Three Poisons" (Aversion, Attachment, and Ignorance) which bind a soul to the cycle of suffering and rebirth. Falling down these cliffs symbolizes the moment the soul is drawn back into a womb for a new life.</note>

# [PART I: THE BARDO OF SEEKING REBIRTH]
<page-num>163</page-num>

Recognize at this moment that you are in the **<term>Sidpa Bardo</term>** <note>original: Sidpa Bardo; the "Intermediate State of Becoming," the final stage of the transition between death and a new rebirth.</note>. Calling upon the **<term>Compassionate One</term>** <note>The Buddha of Compassion, known in Tibetan as Chenrezig and in Sanskrit as Avalokiteshvara.</note> by name, pray with all your heart in this way:

->'O Lord of Compassion, my **<term>Guru</term>** <note>A spiritual teacher or guide.</note>, and the **<term>Precious Trinity</term>** <note>The Three Jewels of Buddhism: the Buddha (the teacher), the Dharma (the teachings), and the Sangha (the community).</note>, do not allow me (state your name) to fall into the unhappy worlds of suffering.'<-

Make sure you do not forget this.

Others, who have lived lives of merit and sincerely practiced their faith, will experience various wonderful pleasures and a great sense of happiness and ease. However, those beings who were "neutral"—those who neither did great good nor created bad **<term>karma</term>** <note>The law of cause and effect, where your past actions shape your current experiences.</note>—will experience neither joy nor pain. Instead, they will feel a kind of dull, colorless indifference. O nobly-born, whatever you experience—even if it is wonderful and pleasant—do not be drawn to it. Do not become obsessed with it. Instead, think: "May my teacher and the Three Jewels be honored with these delights." Let go of all attachment and craving.

Even if you do not feel pleasure or pain, but only a sense of indifference, keep your mind focused and clear. Rest in the state of the **<term>Great Symbol</term>** <note>original: Mahamudra; a profound meditative state where one realizes the true nature of reality.</note>, without thinking about the fact that you are meditating. <note>original: bsgom-med-yengs-med; This refers to "non-meditation and non-distraction." It is a high level of mental focus where the practitioner is so present that they no longer need to "try" to meditate. If you think "I am meditating," that thought itself becomes a distraction.</note> This is of the utmost importance.

O nobly-born, at this time, you may find yourself resting for a short while at bridge-heads, in temples, or by **<term>stūpas</term>** <note>original: mchod-rten (chorten); dome-shaped Buddhist monuments used for prayer and to house sacred relics.</note> of the eight kinds. <note>There are eight traditional styles of these monuments, each commemorating a significant event in the life of the Buddha, such as his birth, enlightenment, or victory over obstacles.</note> However, you will not be able to remain there for very long, for your...
